What is genuinely missing is neither a check nor a limit but a **destination**: `FELD_MAX` was deliberately raised 200 → 4000 → 200000 across three tickets, each time because a real user hit it, and the reasoning recorded there — that only `|` truly breaks a markdown table — is correct about **breaking** and silent about **readability**. A 9.000-character cell breaks nothing and can be read by nobody. The threshold shall therefore be measured against the holding rather than guessed: the longest pre-existing cells are 229 and 153 characters, so 400 lies above everything ever meant as a cell and far below what makes a table unreadable. It shall **not** be a content limit — long text is outsourced and linked, never rejected and never truncated — and `FELD_MAX` shall remain beside it untouched, since it answers a different question. Outsourcing shall be decided **after** cleaning, so that two texts with equal content do not land differently. The outsourced file shall state that it holds the text **as it arrived**, since the line breaks cannot be reconstructed and guessing would be B038. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_pool_langtext.py`: a text above the threshold is outsourced, the cell carries a short text and a link, the file holds the full wording; a short candidate behaves **unchanged** — the counter-test; the boundary itself is not outsourced; line breaks and `|` in the input do not break the table; the outsourced file is in the same commit as the row; a failed commit removes the outsourced file again; `FELD_MAX` is unaffected) | medium | reviewed |
| SWR-125 | The organisation-wide *unterminiert* check shall ask for a **sprint number**, not for a calendar date: an open ticket counts as scheduled if it carries `geplant_sprint`; a ticket carrying only a `frist` shall count as **unscheduled**, and a calendar `frist` on a ticket that is not a `decision-request` shall itself be reported as a finding (`kalenderfrist`). Rationale, measured on 2026-08-17 (letter `pm/N-0041`): the client reported calendar deadlines in the plan table for the second time. The cause is not a slip of one session but this very check. `unterminierte_tickets` (SWR-091 -> SWR-114 -> SWR-117) reports every open ticket **without** `frist`, so every run must invent a date or the preflight goes red — while the opposite has been decided since **SWR-106** (`pm/T-0041`, v1.12: *‚Terminierung auf Sprints statt auf Kalenderdaten‘*), a sentence that also stands in the docstring of the sprint counter itself. The decision changed no check, and for five sprints the check won in silence: **a decision that changed no check is a statement of intent.** Measured rather than assumed while implementing: the line was **printed and never counted** (`befunde += 1` absent at the sprint-10 HEAD), so the preflight stayed green — the first draft of this requirement claimed it turned red, an unverified claim about our own code inside the very item that reworks an unverified figure. The finding is sharpened by the correction: it sufficed that *unterminiert 0* is among the numbers every sprint close **reports** to the client, so **a metric steers as soon as it is reported, even when it blocks nothing**. The unscheduled line shall therefore count as a finding from now on, on the reasoning SWR-122 already recorded verbatim: a line that blocks nothing did not prevent the report of sprint 9. This is the mirror image of SWR-122, where a check had no reader; here a rule had no check. Measured over the holding, all 14 open non-DR tickets carried a `frist` between **+168 and +408 sprints** past the sprint they were planned for (median **+240** at a 60-minute cadence, 24 sprints/day) — a deadline that cannot be missed while the work happens anywhere within a fortnight, and all 14 showed **green** throughout the four undetected deferrals of `pm/T-0039`. The date shall remain exactly where a **human** waits: a `decision-request` is steered by `frist` + `default`, because a human's response time is measured in days and not in 60-minute runs, and a sprint number there would be a promise about the client's calendar. This is the same boundary `sprint_vergangen` (SWR-112) already draws when it exempts decision requests — now drawn identically at both ends. The new finding shall be **reported** and not merely no longer demanded, since an unenforced rule is what produced this defect in the first place; and it shall be counted as a finding, since a line that blocks nothing did not prevent the report of sprint 9 either. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_unterminiert_sprint.py`: a ticket with `geplant_sprint` and no date is scheduled; a ticket with only a `frist` is **unscheduled** — the inversion, and the counter-test that fails against the previous behaviour; a `decision-request` with `frist` stays scheduled and produces **no** `kalenderfrist` finding; a `decision-request` without `frist` is unscheduled; takt tickets are unaffected; `done`/`rejected` are never subjects; a non-DR ticket with a calendar `frist` is reported as `kalenderfrist`; preflight prints both lines at 0 and counts `kalenderfrist` as a finding) | high | reviewed |
| SWR-126 | A letter shall be a **thread of contributions** rather than one question/answer pair: `briefkasten.beitraege` shall split a letter body into ordered contributions, `sende` shall accept an optional target letter (`brief=N-XXXX`) and append a contribution instead of creating a new file, and a contribution by the **human** shall reset the letter's `status` to `offen` in the **same commit** as the appended text. Origin: letter `pm/N-0031` (*'Ich möchte gerne auf meine Fragen und deine Antworten direkt weiter kommentieren'*), whose parent ticket `pm/T-0039` had been deferred **four times with a verbatim identical reason** before being decomposed. The split rule shall be **measured against the holding, not assumed**: across 41 letters there are **52** `## Antwort…` headings — all carrying an ISO date, and 52 > 41 because letters with several team contributions already exist, hand-made, so the client's request describes an existing practice that lacked a tool — and **11** further `##` headings that are *sections inside* an answer (`## 1. Was heute fehlt`, `## 2. Was gebaut wird (pm/T-0040, Frist 23.08.)`), of which exactly **one** carries an ISO date in its trailing bracket (`## Vollzug (Team, 2026-08-16, Routine-Session)` in `pm/N-0015`) and that one **is** a second contribution. A naive 'every `##` starts a contribution' would therefore mis-split **11 letters**; the rule shall be: a contribution heading begins with `Antwort` (the B054 entry point, generalised rather than replaced) **or** carries an ISO date in a trailing bracket. All 41 existing letters shall remain readable **without migration**, the first block before any contribution heading being the human's first contribution, whose sender and time are taken from the frontmatter and **not invented** (B038). `spalte_antwort` shall become a **view on** `beitraege` and not a second parser, since two places splitting one file differently is B033 and B054 is the record of what that costs — its promise of two blocks is unchanged so that the existing chat view keeps working until `pm/T-0060` builds the thread view. The status reset is **not** optional: `offene()` feeds the preflight line and the cockpit tile, so a contribution on an already-answered letter would be seen by **no** session — the client would have written, the system would have accepted, and nobody would have read it. The reset shall be unconditional in this path rather than gated on a sender check, because `COMMIT_IDENTITAET` fixes this path to the human by construction and a sender field can be forged. The target id shall be validated against `N-\d{4}` **before** any path is built from it. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_brief_verlauf.py`: a legacy letter yields exactly two contributions; `pm/N-0015` with its hand-made second team block yields three; the ten section headings inside answers split nothing — **the counter-test over all 41 real letters**, which fails against a naive `##` rule; a new contribution is appended with sender and ISO time; an answered letter goes back to `offen`; text and status land in **one** commit; a failed commit reports 'saved, not committed' and does not lose the contribution; an invalid or unknown id is rejected before writing; `sende` without `brief` is byte-identical to before) | high | reviewed |
| SWR-127 | The pool shall validate the **charter profile** of a team-founding request against the field list decided in `pm/T-0028`: `auftrag` and `grenzen` are mandatory, `profil` and `datenklasse` must each lie within their closed list, `rollen` and `zugaenge` are free text, and a `datenklasse` of `sensibel` or `geheim` shall return an **obligation in plain language** stating that the repository stays without a GitHub remote and carries `.kein-remote`. It shall **not decide**: founding a team is class A and stays with the human. Rationale: `pm/T-0028` had been deferred **four times by exactly one sprint** (`geplant_sprint` 7→8→9→10→11, measured from the field's git history) — the same count at which sprint 10 derived the rule *'too large → decompose, do not defer'* from `pm/T-0039` and then did not apply it to `pm/T-0028` sitting in the same table of the same run. The field list itself was decided in sprint 10 with a reason per field; what was missing is the place where it **holds**, which is precisely what SWR-125 found missing for SWR-106: **a field list no code checks is a statement of intent.** `grenzen` is mandatory because it is the only field in the list where silence carries the *wider* reading — an empty 'limits' field reads as 'no limits'. The obligation shall be a **return value** and not a comment, because a conclusion that does not leave the function is read by nobody (SWR-122). No length limit shall be added beside SWR-124, whose destination-rather-than-number reasoning already answers that question; `FELD_MAX` moved 200 → 4.000 → 200.000 and the right answer was never a number. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_pool_steckbrief.py`: a complete request is accepted with no obligation; each mandatory field missing is rejected naming the field; free-text fields may be empty; a value outside either closed list is rejected **naming the alternatives** (B038); all four data classes are admissible; `sensibel` and `geheim` each produce exactly one obligation mentioning `.kein-remote`, `intern` and `offen` produce none — the counter-test, without which the obligation would be a standing warning; the obligation is part of the result; text is cleaned through the pool's single `_text_bereinigen`; a missing profile is an error and not an empty result; unknown fields are not carried over; a 9.000-character field is not truncated) | high | reviewed |

| SWR-128 | The organisation shall have a **JavaScript test track**, and its state shall be reported by the preflight in **every** run — green, red, or **skipped**. Rationale, measured on 2026-08-17: the holding carried **741 Python tests and zero JS tests** while `app.js` had grown to 1.524 lines and SWR-098/099/100 demand evidence at JavaScript. ADR-002 took that gap **deliberately** for an MVP of 'four views and one form' and named in the same sentence the condition under which it ends — 'reassess as frontend scope grows' — so this requirement does not revoke ADR-002 but redeems it. The track shall follow ADR-008: render **decisions** move into `backend/static/regeln.js`, a file that touches neither DOM nor network and is therefore checkable without a browser, while `app.js` stays the thin part that turns an answer into an element; the runner shall be Node's **built-in** `node --test`, with **no npm, no package.json and no downloaded dependency**, so that ADR-002's 'no build' survives unchanged. Three states shall be distinguished by `js_tests.lauf` — `ok`, `rot`, `uebersprungen` — and **`uebersprungen` shall never pass as `ok`**: that is the SWR-114/SWR-122 lesson verbatim, since a check that did not run is indistinguishable from a green one the moment both report the same thing, and it is exactly how 'zero JS tests' stayed unnoticed for five sprints while the preflight reported STARTKLAR. Red shall count as a finding; **skipped shall not**, because whether Node may become a prerequisite of the project is a **new external tool** and therefore class A — it is put to the human as decision request `projects/p12/T-0007` with deadline and default, and a tool nobody has decided on may not block the human's run. The skip message shall name that decision request, so that the reader learns not only that something did not run but what can be done about it. The separation shall be **held by a check and not by the ADR alone**: a rule that wanders back into `app.js` is unchecked again, and sprint 11 measured three times over what a written rule without a check is worth. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_js_teststrecke.py`: the test files are found; a missing directory yields an empty list rather than an error; the track runs green and counts its tests — a green track with **zero** tests is not a track; **without node the state is `uebersprungen` and explicitly not `ok`**, the message names `node` and `p12/T-0007` — the central counter-test; an empty track reports itself instead of passing as green; a deliberately failing suite really does turn the state red and names the case — the counter-test that the track can go red at all; the preflight prints a JS line even under `--skip-tests`; `regeln.js` contains no DOM or network access **in code**, with comments stripped first, plus the counter-test that stripping comments does not let a real access through; `app.js` reads all four rules from `Regeln` rather than repeating them; `index.html` loads `regeln.js` **before** `app.js`, compared on the `<script>` lines and not on mere mentions) + the JS suite itself (`platform/tests/js/`) | high | reviewed |
| SWR-129 | A letter in the HMI shall be displayed as a **thread**: every contribution with its sender and time, visually distinguishable by author, an own reply field **per letter**, and a letter reopened by the human's follow-up shall be **marked as such**. Origin: `pm/T-0060`, part b of `pm/T-0039` (letter `pm/N-0031`), the display half of SWR-126 which built the thread in the backend. The author of a contribution shall be resolved from the **user registry** (`/api/nutzer`, SWR-037) and not by comparing the sender against the letter's `von` field: the human may pick any registered user when sending, so a second contribution of his own under a different name would be counted as a team answer — the registry is a fact, the comparison would be an assumption (B038). Without a registry the rule shall fall back to the B054 entry point ('a heading beginning with *Antwort* is a team contribution'), which is the rule that carries the 41 legacy letters. The **reopened** marking is the point on which the whole change turns: SWR-126 resets `status` to `offen` when the human appends, so without its own marking such a letter looks exactly like one never answered, and the client cannot see that his follow-up arrived. The per-letter reply field shall be built from the **same** send path as the field for a new letter, differing only in the `brief` parameter (SWR-126) — a second write path beside it would be B033. Where the payload carries no `beitraege` at all, the view shall fall back to `nachricht`/`antwort` rather than render nothing, because an empty view claims there is nothing there. | STK-019 | JS tests (`platform/tests/js/regeln.test.cjs`: the first contribution is always the human's, with and without a registry; a legacy team answer is recognised as team; **a second human contribution under a *different* registered name is human** — the counter-test that fails against the naive 'sender equals von'; `Vollzug (Team, …)` from `pm/N-0015` is team although it does not begin with *Antwort*; without a registry the B054 entry point holds; a letter that is `offen` **with** a team answer is reopened, a fresh letter is **not** and an answered one is **not** — without these two counter-tests the marking would be a standing badge on every open letter; sender and time of the first contribution come from the letter when the fields are empty, and nothing is invented when the letter is empty too; a payload without `beitraege` still yields a readable two-block thread; sorting shows newest first **and leaves the input list untouched** — the counter-test against a bare `reverse()`) | medium | reviewed |
| SWR-130 | After a message is sent, the letterbox view shall show it **without a reload**, and it shall do so **also when the commit failed**. Origin: `pm/T-0058`, the remainder of letter `pm/N-0039` (*'nur bei reload erst angezeigt'*); the misleading message was SWR-121 and its cause SWR-123, this is the display. The view shall reload **immediately** instead of after the previous 900 ms timer, and shall render what `GET /api/briefkasten` delivers rather than assembling the contribution locally — no second content state in the frontend (B033). The failure case is the substantive half: the message is written to disk **before** git runs (SWR-121), so a failed commit never means a lost message, and a list that does not show it claims the opposite. The view shall therefore reload on failure as well and mark that letter **'gespeichert, noch nicht verbucht'** rather than 'failed'. The letter is identified by reading its `N-XXXX` id out of the SWR-121 message, which is precisely why SWR-121 requires the id to be named there; where no id is present **none shall be invented** (B038) — that is a real error (empty text, too long, unknown project) and shall be shown as one. The send button shall stay disabled until the redraw replaces it, unchanged from SWR-102/B055, where an early release produced a second letter on a second click. | STK-019 | JS tests (`platform/tests/js/regeln.test.cjs`: the id is read out of the SWR-121 message; **no id is invented from a message that carries none** and from an empty message — the counter-test without which every error would pass as 'saved'; a `T-XXXX` ticket id is **not** a letter id) + `test_js_teststrecke.py` (`app.js` reads `Regeln.briefIdAusFehler` rather than carrying its own copy) | medium | reviewed |

| SWR-131 | There shall be **exactly one** answer to the question *'is this decision request decided?'*, and every reader shall use it. Origin, measured: on 2026-08-17 at **11:48:25** the client decided `projects/p12/T-0007` via the inbox with `B-node-optional`; the inbox accepted it, wrote it into the ticket body and committed it. In the following sixteen minutes the **same sprint** wrote three reports — sprint plan (11:50), session agenda, project status (12:03) — each stating the question still lay with him, deadline 24.08., and the preflight agreed (*'1 Ticket wartet auf den Menschen: p12/T-0007'*) while reporting STARTKLAR. The cause was **two truths about one word**: `inbox._dr_tickets`/`inbox.historie` read 'decided' from the **body marker** (SWR-039), while `board.wartet_auf_mensch`, `aggregation` and the preflight read it from **`status`** — and `inbox.entscheide` never touches `status`, correctly so under ADR-003, since the human's write path must not advance the team's work state. **A decision in prose is invisible to every check.** The resolution point shall be `board.dr_entschieden`: body marker **or** final status — not two statements about one thing but the beginning and the end of **one** process, which is why both are needed (42 of the 43 decided DRs in the holding carry both, `p12/T-0007` carried only the first). The marker shall be matched at the **start of a line**, so that a body merely *mentioning* a decision ('the decision is still pending') does not count — the same trap as the comment false-positive during SWR-128, where a check punished the very text that explained the state. `inbox.ENTSCHIEDEN` shall **delegate** to that constant rather than keep a copy, and the test shall compare **identity, not value**, because two equal strings in two files are exactly the state that produced the fault. `board.wartet_auf_mensch` shall return false for a decided DR, making the sentence *'a decided DR waits for nobody'* — verbatim in the SWR-120 docstring since sprint 9, **right and ineffective** — effective for the first time. ⚠ That half **alone would be worse than the fault**: the ticket would vanish from every view while still `open`, with follow-up work (booking, unblocking `blocked_by` neighbours) undone and **no reader** — SWR-122 one floor down. The second half is therefore mandatory: a new preflight finding `dr_entschieden_nicht_verbucht` shall name every DR whose decision is present but not booked, count towards `PREFLIGHT: N Befund(e)` and deny STARTKLAR — **naming the tickets, not just a number** (B038, as SWR-114/SWR-120). Legacy DRs on `done` shall **not** trigger it, or the finding would be 42-fold red on the day it is introduced and read as noise within one run. Where a skip message points at a decision request, it shall name the **decision taken** and no longer a deadline nobody is waiting for. ⚠ **'Every reader' shall be held by a count, not by a claim.** The first pass of this requirement converted three readers and missed two, found only by grepping the source afterwards: `aggregation.cockpit` carried a third copy of the marker inline, and `dr_benachrichtigung` a fourth. A test shall therefore assert that **exactly one** definition of the marker literal exists across `backend/` and `scripts/`, or 'one place' would be a statement about the day of introduction rather than about the holding — the SWR-125 lesson, where SWR-106 abolished calendar deadlines and fourteen were back five sprints later. ⚠⚠ **The fourth reader is the one with external effect, and it was the weakest.** `dr_benachrichtigung.unbenachrichtigte` filtered only on `status` and the dispatch marker, never on 'decided', while `warnfaellige` one function below did check it — so the deadline reminder was correct and the *new-DR* mail was not. Measured: `projects/p12/T-0007` carries the decision line of 11:48:25 and below it `**Benachrichtigt:** 2026-08-17 per E-Mail (SWR-033)`, a marker written **only** when `sende()` returns `ok=True` (`mailer.sende` returns `False` without `SMTP_HOST`) — an e-mail announcing a 'new decision request' with 'deadline 2026-08-24' actually went out to the client twelve minutes after he had answered it. A notification about a question its recipient has already answered does more than waste his time: it makes him doubt his answer arrived. ⚠⚠ **And the fault was not unchecked — it was checked and confirmed.** `test_entschiedene_drs_ohne_warnung` asserted `["gesendet"]` for a decided-but-open DR: it verified SWR-034 correctly (no deadline warning) and in the same line demanded the new-DR mail. **A check that certifies the fault is worse than no check**, because it also defends the state against every change; the test shall be renamed, inverted, and paired with a counter-test that the dispatch path is not simply switched off. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_dr_verbuchung.py`: the body marker counts although the status is open; a final status counts; **an undecided DR still waits for the human** — the counter-test that the counter was not simply switched off; **a body that only mentions a decision is not decided**, in two phrasings — the counter-test against punishing the explanatory text; an indented marker counts; a missing `_body` falls back to the status instead of raising; `inbox.ENTSCHIEDEN` **is** `board.ENTSCHEIDUNGSMARKER` by identity; a non-DR with `verantwortlich: mensch` is untouched, so SWR-116 still holds; the finding names the ticket; **a booked DR yields no finding** — the counter-test against 42-fold red; both halves are asserted **on one ticket**, since dropping either yields the old false report or a silent loss; and the **real holding** is asserted clean, not only a tmp_path model — the SWR-128 lesson; **exactly one definition of the marker literal** exists across `backend/` and `scripts/`, counted in the source; `dr_benachrichtigung.ENTSCHIEDEN` and `.FINAL` delegate by identity) + `test_dr_benachrichtigung.py` (**a decided DR receives no mail at all** — the inverted test that previously asserted the opposite; **an undecided DR still receives the new-DR mail** — the counter-test against a silenced dispatch path) + `test_js_teststrecke.py` (the skip message names `B-node-optional` and **no longer the 2026-08-24 deadline**) | high | reviewed |

| SWR-132 | The HMI shall show **all** open tasks of **all** teams and projects on one page, and shall offer the **same** list grouped by role. Origin: letters `pm/N-0038` (*'die offenen aufgaben von den Teams und Projekten priorisieren'*) and `pm/N-0042` (*'aufgaben nach rollen sehen. also Team-Rolle-Aufgaben alle außer geschlossen'*), via `pm/T-0064`, part a of `pm/T-0054`. **The list shall come from the same Python object that already produces `offen_gesamt`** — `sprint.offene_tickets`, reached through `aggregation.projekte`/`projekt_pfad`, so no second discovery path exists. The ticket draft named `aggregation` as the source; measurement found `sprint.offene_tickets` to be the better one precisely because the **count already comes from there**, and a second collection beside it would recreate the state SWR-131 cost this very sprint: two answers to one question with no way for the reader to tell which to trust. Number and list cannot diverge because they are **one object** — that is the assurance, not an intention, and the test asserts the length identity rather than merely that both exist. The list shall **not** be truncated: `aggregation.cockpit` shows `offene[:3]` (SWR-074/094) and that is right for a tile, but wrong here, because the purpose of the list is that the client can prioritise, and a silently shortened list is a second prioritisation beside the one he wants to make himself. Compaction belongs in the **view** (`pm/T-0066`: fold, do not omit), never in the source. `rolle` and `verantwortlich` shall remain **two separate fields** and shall not be merged into one grouping: the professional role (`pl`, `dev`, `cm`, …) and the question *'does the human or the team act?'* are two questions, and merging them was the finding behind SWR-116, where `rolle: mensch` carried a second, behaviour-changing meaning. `verantwortlich` shall be resolved through `board.verantwortlich_wert`, so that the list and the BOARD column do not read an empty field differently. Grouping, sorting and group labelling shall live in `regeln.js` (**ADR-008**), checkable without a browser, and `app.js` shall only turn answers into elements. Every task shall appear in **exactly one** group — appearing twice means counted twice, appearing nowhere means vanished — and a task without a role shall receive the **named** group `'ohne Rolle'` (SWR-096), sorted **last**, because between `cm` and `pl` the absence of a role would look like a role. Sorting shall be by project then id and explicitly **not** by priority or deadline, for the same reason the list is not truncated. Every group label shall carry its **count**, so no entry disappears behind a collapsed block without a number (`pm/N-0025`, `pm/N-0038`). The empty case shall be **named** as a real zero rather than left to look like a failed request (SWR-114/SWR-122/SWR-128). `_ticket` shall not leave the API: it carries the whole ticket body and would be a second route to fields the list already names explicitly. | STK-019 | JS tests (`platform/tests/js/regeln.test.cjs`: sorting by project then id **without mutating the input** — the counter-test against a bare `sort()`; **not sorted by priority or deadline** — the counter-test that the client's prioritisation is not pre-empted; roles alphabetical; **every task in exactly one group**, asserted both for duplicates and for omissions; a task with an empty or whitespace role lands in the **named** group, both in the same one — without `trim()` `'   '` would be an invisibly named role; `'ohne Rolle'` sorts **last**, not alphabetically; the group label always carries the count, including `(0)` and for a null group; **`verantwortlich` does not group** — the counter-test against the SWR-116 merge; empty and missing input yield an empty grouping rather than an error) + unit tests (`test_aufgabenliste.py`: the list spans projects and is **not cut to three** — five tickets yield five; closed tickets are absent; `rolle` and `verantwortlich` are separate keys with the SWR-116 default; `verantwortlich` matches `board.verantwortlich_wert` for an invalid value, so list and BOARD agree; recurring `takt` tickets are included; **`len(offene) == offen_gesamt` on the real holding**; the list carries the fields the role view needs; `_ticket` never appears; `app.js` reads all four rules from `Regeln` and formulates none itself — the counter-test that the rule cannot wander back into the view; the empty case is named) | high | reviewed |

| SWR-133 | The cockpit groups shall be **collapsible**, and collapsing shall **fold, never omit**. Origin: letter `pm/N-0042` (*'mach das Cockpit bischen übersichtlicher, da muss man so viel scrollen -> kompakter'*) via `pm/T-0067`, part a of `pm/T-0066`. ⚠ The requirement is defined by a **contradiction between two letters of the same morning**: `pm/N-0038` (06:20) demands he see **all** open tasks in order to prioritise, `pm/N-0042` (12:00) demands less scrolling. **Resolution: compact means folded, not omitted** — collapsed is not gone, the count stays on the title and one click brings it back, whereas omitting would be a second silent prioritisation beside the one he wants to make, which is the very thing he has objected to twice. The resolution was **disclosed to him in the reply letter** rather than applied as a silent interpretation. The group label shall use the **same** function as the cross-project task list (`Regeln.gruppenTitel`, SWR-132) — one title format, not two (B033). The fold state shall survive a **tab change** (module variable, since the page is not reloaded) and deliberately **not** a restart: a state surviving a restart would have to be explained on return, or a group is missing and nobody knows why. ⚠ `undefined` and `false` shall remain **two different states** (SWR-108: a real zero versus not delivered) — *never touched* means take the group's default, *collapsed* means the human collapsed it; were `undefined` read as `false`, every group would start closed and he would see **less** than before, the opposite of both letters. Compaction shall happen in the **view**, never in the source: `/api/cockpit` shall keep delivering all projects, and a second truncation point beside `offene[:3]` would be B033. ⚠⚠ **The effect shall not be claimed as measured.** `pm/T-0066` DoD 4 asked how many tiles fit at 1920×1080 before and after; that is a question to a **rendered browser**, which ADR-008's DOM-free track deliberately does not have, and a headless browser would be a **new external tool** and therefore class A — not the team's decision (same position as Node, `p12/T-0007`). The convenient move would have been to build the folding and book DoD 4 as done with a number that reads like a measurement and is an estimate (B027/B038); sprint 13 measured that cost at `platform/T-0013`, whose DoD assumed a 60-minute cadence while 7 of 12 intervals were below it. The measurement shall therefore be its own ticket (`pm/T-0068`, `verantwortlich: mensch`, cheapest path first — the client sits in front of the screen in question), taken **once** and cited by both readers, since `projects/p11/T-0008` carries the same open question as risk R2. | STK-019 | JS tests (`platform/tests/js/regeln.test.cjs`: an untouched group takes the group's default, open or closed; **`undefined`/`null` is not `false`** — the counter-test without which every group would start collapsed, asserted both directly and by inequality against an explicitly collapsed state; an explicit state wins over the default in both directions; `gruppenTitel` carries the count for cockpit groups too, from the same function as the task list) | medium | reviewed |

| SWR-134 | There shall be **exactly one** path by which this organisation writes to git, and every writing caller shall use it. ⚠ **The first correction this requirement makes is to its own ticket.** `platform/T-0015` (sprint 13) stated that SWR-123 clears git locks by `unlink` and demanded a fallback to `rename`; measured in sprint 14, that fallback has existed in `preflight.entferne_artefakte` since `pm/T-0023` (2026-08-16) — **building the stated DoD would have changed nothing**. The ticket described a symptom it had not read the code for, which is the same shape as `platform/T-0013`, whose assumed 60-minute cadence its own measurement refuted. The measured fault is different and larger: on this mount **every** git invocation — including a read-only `git status` — leaves a `.git/index.lock` that git may not unlink (`Operation not permitted`), and the **next writing** invocation in that repository fails with `fatal: Unable to create '.git/index.lock': File exists`, exit 128. Of the **eight** places in this organisation that build a `git commit`, exactly **one** — the letterbox — cleared that lock; the other seven ran into the very fault whose repair already lay in the house. > **A repair that only its own site of discovery uses is a repair of the site and not of the fault.** The resolution point shall be `backend/git_schreiben`: `verbuche` for the `add`+`commit` pair, `ruf` for a single command, both clearing **once and not in a loop** through `_einmal_wiederholen`, so that 'once, never a loop' exists as one sentence rather than two copies. Clearing shall happen **only after** an attempt has failed and never pre-emptively: a lock held by a *running* git process must be left alone, and only the failed attempt shows that the lock belongs to nobody this call would disturb — the precondition of SWR-123 is preserved, not widened. The clearing shall **not** be a second mechanism (B033): it delegates to `preflight.finde_lock_artefakte`/`entferne_artefakte`, asserted by an invocation and not by a claim. `stderr` and `stdout` shall stay **separate fields**: the letterbox reports only `stderr` to the human (SWR-121) while `teams.py` must recognise git's 'nothing to commit' — which git writes to **stdout** — and folding both into one string would make one of those two questions unanswerable. A failing repair shall never be worse than none: where clearing raises or clears nothing, the path returns the honest first error unchanged, and the seam `entsperren=` exists so that this assurance can be *tested* rather than merely stated (SWR-125: an assurance without a check is not yet a practice). ⚠ **'Every writer' shall be held by a count, not by a promise** — the SWR-131 lesson, whose first pass converted three readers and missed two. A test shall parse the **syntax tree** of `backend/`, `scripts/` and `orchestrator/` and assert that exactly one file starts a `subprocess.run` carrying both `git` and `commit`. ⚠ It shall read the tree and **not** the text: the first draft of this very check searched for the literal `"commit"` and went red on three sites that were all correct — two dictionary keys and the tick's own `git()` helper, which now goes *through* the path — i.e. it would have punished exactly the callers that were migrated properly, the same trap as the comment false-positive in SWR-128 and the line false-positive in `test_org_kopfblock`, for the third time. The counter-direction shall be asserted as well (each of the seven writers still *imports* the path), because the count alone would pass a file that deleted its commit instead of migrating it — a silent loss, which is the SWR-131 warning one floor down. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_git_schreibweg.py`: a commit blocked by a planted `index.lock` succeeds and reports that it retried; **the ordinary path does not clear at all** — the counter-test against clearing on every write; clearing is attempted **exactly once**; a clearing that **raises** leaves the honest first error and no retry; a clearing that finds nothing likewise; 'nothing to commit' is recognised from **stdout** and a real failure is **not** mistaken for it; `ruf` clears and retries for single commands too and returns command output on stdout; a path passed as a plain string is not split into characters; `entsperre` **invokes** `preflight.finde_lock_artefakte`; **`preflight` already falls back to renaming when `unlink` is refused** — the ticket's DoD 1 asserted against the holding instead of built; **exactly one** file builds a git-commit call, counted over the syntax tree; the counter-test that the count would notice a relapse; and the counter-test that it does **not** flag dictionary keys, the tick's `git()` helper, or a read-only `git status`) | high | reviewed |
| SWR-135 | The dashboard shall be its **own tab** that is allowed to leave the global 62rem reading corridor, and the exception shall sit **at the view**. ⚠ **Sprint 24 cut this requirement back to its layout half** (`projects/p11/T-0015`, executing decision `p11/T-0014`, option B); the tile half is recorded below and is no longer required. Origin: `projects/p11/T-0010`, part a of `p11/T-0008` — deferred **four times** and promised to the client in sprint 11. The trigger was a **measurement**, not an idea: `pm/T-0068` counted, on two screenshots of the client's own 4K screen, that **three** project cards are visible without scrolling while roughly a fifth of the width lies empty on each side, because cards are stacked one per row inside the corridor. **The scrolling came from the layout, not from the amount** — which also refuted the assumption behind `pm/T-0066`, whose folding was built for the wrong cause and is nevertheless right. `main { max-width: 62rem }` shall stay **without any special case** and the exception shall be its **own** rule (`main.breit`) — ADR-P11-002 and DR `p11/T-0006` (LAY-a, decided 2026-08-17 08:11), pending unbuilt for five sprints; a rule with an exception list forces every future view to ask *does it apply here?*, and the answer would then not live in the view. ⚠ The width shall be **cleared on every regular draw** inside `zeige()`, or `breit` stays attached after one dashboard visit and every following view loses the corridor — the exception would be global in fact while LAY-b was explicitly rejected, and every future view would otherwise have to know about it. The empty case shall be **named**: *nothing there* and *not loaded* shall not look alike (SWR-114). ⚠ **The effect shall be re-measured, not claimed**: how many tiles fit is a question to a rendered browser, and only the client has one. ⚠⚠ **What was cut in sprint 24, and why it is recorded rather than deleted.** The requirement originally also demanded a route `GET /api/dashboard` that formed compact **project tiles** from `cockpit_alle` with `{wert, zustand}` per field. In sprint 17 the client named those tiles a duplicate of the cockpit (*„ist an sich das gleiche wie das cockpit"*); SWR-148 removed their rendering, `p11/T-0011` built the widget configuration on `/api/widgets`, and `p11/T-0014` decided on 2026-08-17 to retire the route as well. **An endpoint lives from an intention, not from a leftover.** ⚠ The three-state rule itself (`_zustand`, `nicht_geliefert` vs. `echte_null`) did **not** go with it: it carries the cockpit's `zustaende` block since SWR-146, and a cleanup that took it along would have pulled the ground from under an accepted requirement. | STK-021 | Unit tests (`test_dashboard_endpunkt.py::AnsichtTest`: the global corridor rule is unchanged and the exception is its own rule; the width is cleared **inside `zeige()`**, asserted within that function's text and not merely somewhere in the file; the view reads all rules from `Regeln` and formulates none; the dashboard is its own tab; the empty case is named; **the named legacy of three inline copies of the state rule in `cockpitKarte` is held at 0** — `platform/T-0016`, kept as a guard against return rather than deleted) + (`test_dashboard_endpunkt.py::RueckbauTest`, the **guard against the return of the tile half**: `aggregation.dashboard` and `KACHEL_FELDER` are gone, the route is no longer wired in `server.py` — two places, two assertions, since a module can be empty while the route still stands — and ⚠⚠ the **counter-check** that `_zustand`/`zustaende_von` are still there and still separate `nicht_geliefert` from `echte_null`, without which the guard would be green after a clear-cut as well) | high | reviewed |
| SWR-136 | The sprint register shall know an **end**, and no sprint shall be opened while another one is still running. Origin, measured twice on one day: `SESSION-BEFUND-2026-08-17-1105-nebenlaeufigkeit.md` (a routine session started at **10:25** while sprint 11 had been running since **10:04**; ten commits of the neighbour were counted *during* the own runtime) and `SESSION-BEFUND-2026-08-17-1339-nebenlaeufigkeit.md` (**13:19** against the sprint registered at **13:31**). The second time it was no longer harmless: **both runs gave their new requirement the number SWR-134**, and the collision was found by reading the other run's commit, **not by any check** — the rule of 2026-08-16 required the next free **ticket id** to be verified against HEAD and said nothing about `SWR-xxx`, `D0xx`, `N-xxxx`, `L-…` or `ADR-…` (`L-2026-08-17ae`). ⚠ It went well only because the two runs happened to touch different files — luck, not provision. `sprint_register.beginne()` was idempotent **per run identity**, which prevents the *same* run from counting twice and has no case for **two different** runs in one cadence: **a register without an end can not see overlap — it measures openings, not runnings.** A line **without `ende`** shall therefore be a running sprint; `beende(kennung)` shall close it, idempotently, and shall answer `None` for an unknown identity rather than silently create one (B038). `beginne()` shall **refuse** while a sprint runs, and the refusal shall **name** the running sprint and its identity, so the rejected run can report it instead of merely failing (B038, as SWR-114/SWR-120); the refusal shall be distinguishable from a program error at the exit code, since a caller must tell the two apart. ⚠⚠ The refusal shall carry **no time limit**, and this is a **measured** correction of the ticket's own first DoD: over 12 intervals the median was 57 min, the **minimum 15 min**, and **7 of 12** lay below the cadence — the planned bound ('less than one cadence') would have rejected the majority of regular follow-up runs. The abandonment of a dead run shall instead be decided by **write traces**: `beginne()` shall measure the heads of all repos, append the measurement as an observation and refuse; the **next** run shall compare — a moved trace means the other run is working (refuse again), an unmoved trace means nothing has been written since the observation, and then the sprint shall count as abandoned and be taken over. A clock can not make this distinction: after 15 minutes it sees the same value for a working run as for a crashed one. The wait is thereby bounded at **one** cadence — a run that dies shall not lock the routine for ever, and a run that **works** shall not be overtaken. ⚠ `ende` shall be written **also in the abandonment case** (with `abgebrochen: true`), or every second line would lack `ende` and the check would become a standing warning nobody reads. ⚠ The register file shall stay **append-only**, so `ende`, `abgebrochen` and `spur` shall arrive as **their own lines** carrying the same `kennung` and be folded into the sprint on read: rewriting an existing line is the more convenient path and the only one that loses data when two writers meet — precisely the case this module exists for, and an append of one short line is indivisible under `O_APPEND` while a rewrite is not. ⚠ The write-trace measurement shall use **no `subprocess`**, reading heads from disk instead: SWR-134 measured that even a **reading** `git status` leaves an `index.lock` that can not be removed on this mount, so a concurrency check that creates locks would be its own damage case. An unreadable head shall count as **no** movement, or a read error would block every takeover and look like life. The preflight shall **read and report** the register state on every run — per SWR-122 whoever builds a check decides in the same move who reads its result — and shall count a closed sprint without `ende` as a finding, **naming it**; the **running** sprint shall be exempt (its own line carries no `ende` by nature) and so shall sprints before sprint 15, or the check would start fourteen-fold red on the day of its introduction and train looking away — the same trap as the 42 legacy DRs in SWR-131. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_sprint_register_ende.py`: a line without `ende` is a running sprint; `beende` folds, is idempotent and invents nothing for an unknown identity; **the file is only ever appended to** — asserted as the byte prefix being unchanged, the assurance the whole construction rests on; a second run in the same cadence is **refused** and the message names the running sprint; the refusal appends the observation; **counter-tests**: a regular follow-up run after `beende` is **not** refused even 1 minute later, 10 days without `ende` are **still** refused (no clock in either direction), a moved trace refuses again while an unmoved one hands over, `ende` is written **in** the abandonment case, the own identity is never refused; the trace moves on a commit in **any** repo, an unreadable head is no movement, and a **syntax-tree count** asserts the module imports no `subprocess`; the running sprint and the pre-cutoff legacy are **not** findings while a real gap is; the preflight prints the state **always** and counts the gap) + the inverted assurance in `test_sprint_planung.py` (`test_beginne_zaehlt_hoch` **asserted the faulty behaviour** until sprint 15 and now ends the predecessor first) | high | reviewed |
| SWR-137 | Run telemetry shall be **measured, never estimated**, and a missing input shall be **named** instead of filled in. Origin: `promt-team/T-0004`, part **a** of `promt-team/T-0001` — a ticket at its **fifth** deferral whose seam had been written into it verbatim since sprint 12 (*'(a) collect and write per run, (b) evaluate and report per role'*) and was cut along that seam rather than a newly invented one. The client's own role brief forbids estimating twice literally: *'measure tokens per artefact, do not estimate'* and *'if an input is missing it shall be noted in the report as a blocker and not estimated'*. ⚠ Measured at the holding **before** any code: of 7 runs in `p0/management/runs/run-registry.jsonl`, **0** carry any token field, **1** carries an `aufgaben_typ`, `provider` is present in **5**, and `kosten_eur` is `0.0` in **all 7**. Two findings follow and both determine the construction: the field the evaluation needs **exists in the schema and is empty** — *a mandatory field without a check is a wish* — and `kosten_eur: 0.0` means **two different things**, a real measurement for a local Ollama run and 'never collected' for a session exchange, which is SWR-108's cockpit fault one floor down in the raw measurement. There shall therefore be **one** field contract for run telemetry (`token_statisch`, `token_dynamisch`, `kosten_eur`, `dauer_s`) delivering `{wert, zustand}` per field with the **same three state names as the widget contract**, **imported** and not transcribed — asserted by **identity, not equality**, because two equal strings in two files are exactly the state that cost SWR-131, and a fourth spelling of 'keine Daten' is the construction `platform/T-0016` holds as legacy. Missing mandatory inputs shall appear **by name** in `blocker` (B038, as SWR-114/SWR-120) and a `0` shall **never** be a blocker — a free Ollama run really costs nothing, and reporting a measurement as a gap is the same error backwards. ⚠⚠ **The three-state rule shall NOT apply to keys.** It is written for **measurements**: zero cost, zero tokens and an empty artefact list are answers, whereas there is no task type called 'nothing'. The first draft applied it to all mandatory fields and would have waved through **the very finding this requirement exists for** (`aufgaben_typ` empty in 6 of 7 runs) — *applying a rule about measured values to a key is a category confusion, and it hides the gap it is meant to expose.* Keys (`rolle`, `aufgaben_typ`, `provider`) shall therefore count as missing when empty, measurements only when absent. The enrichment shall sit at the **single** write path into the run registry (`guardrails.schreibe_run`) and not in its callers — the lesson of SWR-134, where seven of eight git writers ran without the repair that already existed in the house; the import shall be made **inside** the function so a gateway without `backend` on the path still records the run, since the registry duty outweighs its enrichment. `Ergebnis` shall carry the token fields with default **`None`, not `0`**: an executor that reports nothing has not consumed zero tokens, it has not measured — and a `0` there is the most expensive kind of estimate, because it looks like a result. Existing runs shall stay readable and shall **not** be retro-filled. ⚠ This requirement delivers **no number**; it makes the measurement possible and the gap visible. The collection at the source and the per-role evaluation are `promt-team/T-0005`, deliberately in a different module, because an evaluation living beside the collection could bridge a missing figure silently and nobody would see it. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_telemetrie.py`: a reported `0` is `echte_null` **and** an absent field is `nicht_geliefert`, with the two states asserted **unequal**; the state names are asserted **identical** to `aggregation`'s; `zustaende` reads `FELDER` and no second list; `blocker` names the fields and **a `0` is not a blocker**; an empty `aufgaben_typ` **is** one; ⚠ the sharpest assurance puts **both cases in one entry** — separate tests were each greenable by shifting the rule either way; `ergaenze` leaves the original dict untouched and invents no absent field; `schreibe_run` appends telemetry and blockers and the registry stays append-only, asserted as an unchanged byte prefix; `Ergebnis` defaults to `None`; and the holding itself is **recounted rather than quoted** — 0 of 7 runs carry tokens, at least one lacks `aufgaben_typ`, and the test fails the day that changes so the ticket gets corrected instead of the number ageing silently in a report) | high | reviewed |
| SWR-138 | The HMI shall offer **two** sections at the place of the inbox — *'Für dich: Entscheidungen'* and *'Für dich: Handlungen'* — and a ticket shall appear in **exactly one** of them. Origin: `pm/T-0052`, part e of `pm/T-0038` from letter `pm/N-0031`, at its **fifth** touch. The client asked *'why are the tasks not in the inbox when they are addressed to humans?'* and the answer is that the inbox does not **reject** them, it does not **know** them: it shows undecided `decision-request`s (SWR-039/042), and a ticket carrying `verantwortlich: mensch` with `typ: problem` falls through every one of its filters. **No filter was missing — the channel was.** ⚠ Two sections and not one list: the decision list carries the buttons (`optionen`/`default`/`frist`, SWR-042), so an entry without options there would mean either buttons that do nothing or a list where some entries have buttons and some do not — one surface with two meanings, which is B033, the same trap as the `rolle` field. The section a card belongs to shall be decided by an explicit `knoepfe` flag per section and **not** by its heading: whoever decides the button question from the label has answered it twice as soon as the label changes. The data shall be **one** source: the handlings list shall be the subset of `wartet_auf_mensch` **without** the decision requests, formed in the backend at a single place — the same construction as the role view in SWR-132 (*'a grouping of the same list, not a second view'*), and a test shall assert the subset relation, or the cockpit header (SWR-120) would show a different number from this section. ⚠⚠ A ticket that satisfies **both** conditions — a `decision-request` that also carries `verantwortlich: mensch` — shall belong to the **decisions**, because that is where its options, deadline and default hang; showing it in both would ask for it twice, and the precedence shall be fixed rather than left to whichever field happens to be set. The condition *'waits for the human'* shall be **read from `board.wartet_auf_mensch`** and not restated here — the board column (SWR-119), the cockpit header (SWR-120) and this section ask one question — and this shall be held by a check over the **source**, since a copy of the condition does not show up in the result while both spellings agree, exactly the state that cost SWR-131. `geplant_sprint` shall be delivered as a **number** via `board.parse_sprint_nr`, because SWR-106 permits three spellings and the view is the wrong place to know that. Closed and rejected tickets shall not appear — a decided DR waits for nobody. ⚠ **Both sections shall be rendered even when empty**, each with its own empty text: a section that vanishes at zero is indistinguishable from one that was never built, the same reasoning that makes the preflight print its zero lines (SWR-114/SWR-122); the client shall be able to see that we looked. Section headings, the two empty texts and the button flag shall live in `regeln.js` and be checked without a browser (ADR-008). ⚠ The seam named in the ticket for a decomposition (*between 'Entscheidungen' and 'Handlungen'*) was **measured and found obsolete** at cutting time: the decisions section has existed since SWR-042, so a split would have produced an empty part — a named seam can expire, and that finding belongs in the ticket rather than a silent whole-closure. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_fuer_dich.py`: a `problem` with `verantwortlich: mensch` appears; a `decision-request` does **not**; ⚠ a ticket satisfying **both** goes to the decisions — the only case where the two sections could quarrel; a plain team task appears in neither; `done`/`rejected` are excluded; the empty list is a list and not an error; **the handlings are a subset of `wartet_auf_mensch`**, asserted as a set relation; the card carries `ref`/`titel`/`rolle` and the sprint **as a number**; and a **syntax-tree check** asserts the function calls `board.wartet_auf_mensch` instead of restating the condition) + JS tests (`regeln.test.cjs`: exactly two sections in fixed order; only the decisions section carries buttons, asserted as **unequal**; both sections render when empty with **different** empty texts; ⚠ missing lists become empty ones rather than `undefined`, so one failing API call yields an empty section and not a blank page; entries are passed through unsorted, since a silent ordering would be a second prioritisation beside the client's) | medium | reviewed |
| SWR-139 | A **status change shall be one operation**: whoever writes the new state shall book it in the same move, and if the booking fails the change shall count as **not having happened**. Origin: `platform/T-0017`, a finding a run made about **itself** — the damage case occurred **twice in one run** (sprint 15) and was committed once. Today a change consists of two calls (`board.py <repo> status …` writes the file, a separate `<commit>` books it); if the second fails at an orphaned `.git/index.lock`, the state stands in the file and **not** in the history, the next change overwrites it, and a step is missing from the record — `pm/T-0052` went `in_progress -> done` in the history for exactly this reason. ⚠ The cause is **neither** the lock **nor** inattention: SWR-134 already built the one write path that clears and retries. The fault lies one floor up — *a state change made of two operations has an intermediate state, and every intermediate state nobody books is a lost state.* `board.setze_status` shall therefore book through `backend.git_schreiben` itself; the caller shall pass a **message** and shall not build a commit, because a repair the caller must apply is not a repair (the lesson of SWR-134, one field over, where seven of eight git writers ran without the fix that already lived in the house). ⚠⚠ **The counter-test is the more important half**: a change that alters the file and only *reports* the error is precisely the state this requirement exists against, so on a failed booking the ticket file **and** `BOARD.md` shall be restored to their pre-change bytes and the failure raised. A booking shall be attempted **only** when a message is given: a call without one shall write as before and shall **not** silently commit, or every existing caller (the orchestrator tick, `feedback_route`) would start committing in the middle of its own transaction. ⚠ **No second change on an unbooked one.** If the ticket file in the working copy differs from `HEAD`, `setze_status` shall refuse and the message shall **name the unbooked status** (B038, as SWR-114/SWR-120) — refusing without saying what is in the way merely moves the puzzle. A ticket **not yet in `HEAD`** (newly created) is **not** a finding, or creating and moving a ticket in one run would be impossible; and the check shall be skippable for the caller that is itself mid-transaction, since the orchestrator writes three states inside one branch. The preflight shall report unbooked **status** states as a finding of their own — SWR-110 already reads the working copy, what is missing is the sharpening onto `status`, and per SWR-122 whoever builds a check decides in the same move who reads it. ⚠⚠ **Side finding on SWR-134, measured in the same run: the retry does not help against a *self-inflicted* lock.** `verbuche()` calls `add` and then `commit`; on this mount the **own `add`** leaves the `index.lock` at which the following `commit` fails, and `_einmal_wiederholen` clears, repeats the **pair** — and runs into the same sequence, because the lock arises anew on every attempt (measured at three commits of sprint 15 that answered `FEHLER | geraeumt: 19` and only passed after a separate lock run). The clearing shall therefore happen **between** `add` and `commit`, and it shall be permitted there for a reason that must be stated rather than assumed: **a successful `add` is the proof that no lock was there when it started**, so the lock lying there afterwards is demonstrably the own one. The rejected alternative *'clear preventively before every call'* (DoD 2 of `platform/T-0015`) stays rejected — it has no such proof and would move the lock of a **running** process. A **failed** `add` shall clear nothing. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_statuswechsel.py`: a change with a message writes **and** books, asserted as **one** new commit; a failed booking leaves the ticket file **and** `BOARD.md` byte-identical to before **and** raises — the counter-test the requirement exists for; two changes in a row yield **two** commits; a call **without** a message books nothing, so the existing callers keep their behaviour; a ticket whose working copy differs from `HEAD` is **refused** and the message contains the **unbooked** status; a ticket absent from `HEAD` is not refused; `head_pruefen=False` bypasses the check for the mid-transaction caller; `unverbuchte_status` names ticket, file state **and** HEAD state, is empty when there is none, and an **edited body without a status change is not a finding** — the sharpening onto `status` is the point, since a finding on every edit would be permanently red mid-work and train looking away; and a **syntax-tree check** asserts the preflight actually reads it, per SWR-122) + (`test_git_schreibweg.py`: after a successful `add` the clearing runs **between** `add` and `commit`, asserted at the **call order**; after a **failed** `add` it does **not** run — the two halves of the ownership proof, in separate assurances because a single one is greenable by shifting the rule either way) | high | reviewed |
| SWR-140 | The per-role evaluation of run telemetry shall report **every figure together with its coverage**, and a run that can not be assigned shall be **named, never dropped**. Origin: `promt-team/T-0005`, part **b** of `promt-team/T-0001` — the seam named in the ticket since sprint 12 (*'(a) collect and write per run, (b) evaluate and report per role'*), and the answer the client has been waiting for since letter `promt-team/N-0001` (*'which roles run via Claude, which via Ollama?'*). SWR-137 built the field contract and measured the holding: of 7 runs **0** carry any token field and **6** carry no `aufgaben_typ`. ⚠⚠ The finding that determines the construction is what an average would do to that holding: **a mean over the runs that happened to report is not a mean over the runs**, and printed without its denominator it is indistinguishable from a complete measurement — the same error as `kosten_eur: 0.0` (SWR-137) one level up, at the aggregate instead of the field. Every aggregate shall therefore carry `n_gemessen` **and** `n_gesamt`, and a figure with `n_gemessen == 0` shall be reported as **absent** (`nicht_geliefert`) rather than as `0` — the three state names shall be **imported** from `aggregation`, not restated, for the reason SWR-137 gives. The Soll/Ist comparison per task type shall read the planned chain from `process/roles/registry.yaml` (resolution order per the file's own schema: `script_tasks` before `aufgaben_typen.<typ>` before the role default) and compare it to the `provider` actually used; ⚠ a run **without** `aufgaben_typ` shall appear under `nicht_zuordenbar` **with its ticket id**, because dropping it would shrink the denominator and make the coverage look better than it is — precisely the gap this requirement exists to expose. A provider that is **in** the planned chain but not its first stage shall count as `abweichend_mit_grund` and not as a violation: the chain is a fallback ladder (`on_unavailable: next_in_chain`), so ollama-then-claude running on claude is the ladder working, not a breach — collapsing the two would produce a red report about correct behaviour and train looking away (the trap of SWR-131). Blockers shall be **read** from `telemetrie.blocker` and **not re-derived** (B033); the evaluation shall read the run registry as its **only** source. ⚠ The evaluation shall live in a module **separate** from the collection, per SWR-137's own reasoning: an evaluation beside the collection could bridge a missing figure silently. ⚠ This requirement, too, delivers no baseline — it delivers the **statement that there is none**, per role and per task type, with names. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_auswertung.py`: an aggregate over runs where **none** reports is `nicht_geliefert` and **not** `0`; an aggregate where **some** report carries `n_gemessen` **less than** `n_gesamt` — the sharpest assurance, since a partial figure without its denominator is the error the requirement is named for; a reported `0` counts as **measured** and raises `n_gemessen`, the counter-direction; a run without `aufgaben_typ` lands in `nicht_zuordenbar` **with its ticket** and is **still counted** in `n_gesamt`; the planned chain resolves `script_tasks` before `aufgaben_typen` before the role default; a provider inside the chain but not first is `abweichend_mit_grund` while one **outside** the chain is a deviation; blockers are taken from `telemetrie.blocker` — asserted by a **syntax-tree check** that the module calls it instead of restating the condition; and the holding is **recounted rather than quoted**, so the day a run reports tokens the test fails and the ticket gets corrected instead of the number ageing in a report) | high | reviewed |
| SWR-141 | The token baseline shall be **measured at the source**, and where an API reports only one number the split shall be obtained by **two measurements and a subtraction** — never by apportioning. Origin: `promt-team/T-0005` DoD 1, the half of the seam at which it becomes testable whether the figure **arrives**: SWR-137 built the field contract and measured that **0 of 7** runs carry any token field, which is a write path whose result nobody produces. Ollama's `/api/chat` answers `prompt_eval_count` for the **whole** prompt, while the contract asks for **two** figures, static and dynamic. Splitting that one number by character or word share would be an estimate, and the client's role brief forbids estimating in as many words. The executor shall therefore issue a **second call without generation** (`num_predict: 0`) carrying **only** the system message; its `prompt_eval_count` is the static share including its own template wrapper, and the dynamic share is the difference to the full call — so both figures are measured and their sum is by construction the measured total. ⚠⚠ If **either** measurement is missing, **both** fields shall stay `None`: half a measurement presented as a whole one is SWR-140's error in miniature, and it would be indistinguishable from a complete one in the report. A negative difference shall likewise yield `None` twice — it proves the measurement is broken, and a broken measurement is a gap, not a number. A measured **`0` shall be reported**, being a result. If the main call reports no total the probe shall **not** be made at all: a call whose result nobody can use should not be made. The figures shall be passed through `gateway.core` to the run registry **without a default** — a `roh.get('token_statisch', 0)` is exactly the place where a missing measurement becomes a measured zero, the fault the holding shows seven times over as `kosten_eur: 0.0`. ⚠ The price is one extra prompt evaluation per run; it is local and free, and it is the difference between a baseline and a guess. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_token_messung.py`: static and dynamic **sum to the measured total**; the probe carries only the system message and generates nothing, asserted at the payload; a missing probe yields `None` **twice** — the sharpest assurance; a negative difference likewise; a measured `0` **is** reported; without a total **no second request is made at all**, asserted at the call count; and two **syntax-tree** checks on `gateway.core` — the fields are passed to `Ergebnis`, and no `get` on a `token_` key carries a default. ⚠ The second of those was first written as a **text** search and went red on the **explanation of that very fault** in the comment beside it: a text search can not tell a warning from its subject) | high | reviewed |
| SWR-142 | A gold-set case shall be **machine-readable**, and the way its failure is recognised shall be a **field, not prose**. Origin: `promt-team/T-0006`, part **a** of `promt-team/T-0002` at its **fifth** touch, cut at the seam named in the ticket since sprint 13 — and the seam carries substance rather than convenience: *without a format, twenty cases are twenty individual opinions*, and an eval gate that compares forms instead of results measures the diligence of whoever wrote them. Each case shall be one JSONL line (append-only, like the run registry) carrying `rolle`, `aufgaben_typ`, `eingabe`, `erwartetes_ergebnis` and **`fehlschlag_erkannt_an`**. ⚠⚠ `fehlschlag_erkannt_an` shall be a **structured** check (`{art, wert}` with `art` from a closed set — `enthaelt`, `enthaelt_nicht`, `regex`, `json_pfad`, `datei_existiert`), and a case missing it shall be **rejected** rather than filled with a default: a case whose failure is recognised by *'you can see it'* is not a test case, it is an opinion with a deadline, and a default here would silently turn every unwritten check into a passing one. The closed set shall be closed **in one place** and the validator shall read **it** — a second spelling of the same list is the construction that cost SWR-131. ⚠ Each case shall carry `soll_scheitern_auf` naming the provider stage on which it is **expected to fail**: without it a green eval only proves the task was easy. The field alone is not enough — the validator shall assert that **per task type at least one** case sets it, since a field without a check is a wish (SWR-125, whose lesson this applies rather than cites). ⚠ Sensitive data shall be **named and omitted, never invented as an anonymisation**: a fabricated case measures the fabricator, and `sensibel_ausgelassen` shall carry the reason so the gap in the set is visible instead of looking like completeness. Validation shall report **all** faults of a case at once, not the first: a case corrected five times through five runs is the cost of a validator that stops at the first fault. This requirement delivers **no cases** (`promt-team/T-0007`) and touches no prompt. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_goldset.py`: a complete case passes; a case **without** `fehlschlag_erkannt_an` is **rejected** and **not** defaulted — the counter-test the requirement exists for; prose instead of a structured check is rejected, and so is an `art` outside the closed set, while every member of that set is accepted, asserted by **reading the set** rather than restating it; a set in which **no** case of a task type sets `soll_scheitern_auf` is rejected **naming the task type**, while one case suffices; a case with `sensibel_ausgelassen` **without** a reason is rejected, since an unexplained gap is indistinguishable from completeness; all faults of a case are reported **together**, asserted at a case with three; and the file stays append-only, asserted as an unchanged byte prefix) | high | reviewed |
| SWR-143 | A repair shall find its own dependencies **relative to itself**, never by guessing at the caller's import path. Origin: `platform/T-0018`, a defect of SWR-134 **measured in production during sprint 16**: `git_schreiben.entsperre` put `os.path.dirname(__file__)` on `sys.path` — which is `backend/`, not `scripts/` — so `import preflight` failed, the function returned **0**, and the lock clearing that SWR-134 exists to spread **did not run at all**, unless the caller happened to have `scripts/` on the path already. `board`, `preflight` and the test file itself all do, which is why it went unseen for a whole sprint. ⚠⚠ The failure mode is the worst kind available to this construction, because it is **invisible in the test suite and visible only under load**: the assurance `test_entsperre_benutzt_den_preflight_mechanismus` was green throughout, since the test module puts `scripts/` on the path at import time. > **The repair worked everywhere the caller brought it along — which is precisely where SWR-134 did not need it.** The module shall therefore derive the path from its own location (`../scripts`), and the assurance shall **remove** `scripts/` from `sys.path` **and** the cached module from `sys.modules` before measuring: a test that leaves the runner's path in place measures the runner, not the function. ⚠ The general rule this instance stands for: whenever a module reaches for another by manipulating `sys.path`, the anchor shall be `__file__` and the resulting path shall be **normalised**, since a relative anchor makes the behaviour depend on the working directory of whoever imported first. | STK-019 | Unit test (`test_git_schreibweg.py::test_entsperre_findet_preflight_ohne_zutun_des_aufrufers`: with `scripts/` removed from `sys.path` and `preflight` evicted from `sys.modules`, `entsperre` still clears a lock — the assurance fails on the old code and passes on the new, which is the only way to tell a repair from a description of one) | high | reviewed |

| SWR-144 | A one-click termination shall change **exactly one field**, shall derive that field's new value **at write time from the register** and never from what the client saw, and shall report *'nothing changed'* as a **success** — never as a failure. Origin: `pm/T-0065`, part b of `pm/T-0054` from letter `pm/N-0038` (*'der Mensch priorisiert offene Aufgaben aller Teams/Projekte fuer den naechsten Durchlauf — Knopf statt Einzelbearbeitung'*), at its **fourth** touch. The ticket states in its own text that it has **no seam** and that inventing one would be worse than building it, so the answer at the fourth touch is a decision, not a fourth deferral. ⚠ The substance is DoD 4 and not the button: `board.aktualisiere` raises `ValueError('keine Aenderung')` when the field already carries the value, and `tickets.speichere` mapped **every** `ValueError` to HTTP **400** — so a ticket that was **already** terminated answered with the same shape as a rejected write. > **A button whose 'already done' and whose 'did not work' are indistinguishable is a display without a statement.** The unchanged case shall therefore return HTTP **200** with an explicit `unveraendert: true` and **no commit**, since a commit without a change would put an event into the history that did not happen. ⚠ The second half is why the button may read its own fingerprint although SWR-080 exists: SWR-080 protects against a **stale form** being written back, and the protected quantity is the value the client saw. A termination carries **no** client value — its only value is the next sprint number, taken from the register **inside** the write — so there is nothing stale to overwrite. ⚠ That argument holds **only** as long as the change is exactly one field, which is why the assurance measures the **field set** and not the outcome: the day a second field joins, the fingerprint argument silently becomes false. ⚠ `prio` stays untouched (`pm/T-0054`): *'for the next run'* is a statement about the date, not about importance. | STK-005 | Unit tests (`test_terminieren.py`: (1) the button sets `geplant_sprint` to `aktuell()+1` and touches no other field — asserted against the **diff of the front matter**, not against the return value; (2) a ticket already on the next sprint answers `ok: true, unveraendert: true`, its file is **byte-identical** afterwards and **no** commit was made; (3) a failing commit leaves ticket and BOARD.md byte-identical and reports in plain text; (4) the origin note `Mensch via HMI` stands in the ticket **and** in the commit) | high | reviewed |

| SWR-145 | A tool whose **incomplete** mode writes to the location of the **complete** one shall not exist: where a generator discovers its own inputs, discovery shall be the **default** and the narrowing shall be the flag. Origin: `platform/T-0019`, triggered by sprint 17 **in this run** — `trace_matrix.py` was called without `--alle-projekte`, read only `p0`, and overwrote the canonical `swr-test-matrix.md` with **24 SWRs / 101 gaps** where sprint 16 had left **143 / 0**; it removed 121 lines, printed the words *'Matrix geschrieben'* and exited **0**. ⚠ Neither default is wrong on its own — `--alle-projekte` is off because a flag adds, and `--ziel` is the canonical file because the normal case should need no arguments — which is exactly why no existing check found it. > **A tool whose incomplete mode writes to the place of the complete one turns a typo into a total finding.** ⚠⚠ This is the construction of SWR-143 one floor up: there a function found its dependency only if the **caller** brought it along, here a tool finds its sources only if the caller brings a flag — and in both cases the result of an unknowing caller is **not recognisable as wrong**. The requirement is therefore: absent an explicit `--swr`, the sources come from the same discovery as board and cockpit (SWR-070); an explicit `--swr` still wins, because it is the only place where the caller demonstrably meant something else; and `--alle-projekte` keeps working, because `abschluss.cmd` and CI pass it — a repair may not break the caller who was right. ⚠ The counter-direction is part of the requirement and not a nicety: an explicit `--swr` shall find **exactly** that one file, otherwise DoD 1 would be satisfiable by 'always read everything' and the product mode (`--produkt`, own SWR file **and** own target) would be silently broken. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_trace_matrix.py`: (1) a call with **no** flags against a stock holding requirements in **two** projects finds **both** — measured on the number of requirements, not on the exit code, because the old behaviour also exited 0; (2) an explicit `--swr <one file>` finds exactly that one; (3) `--alle-projekte` continues to behave as before) | high | reviewed |

| SWR-146 | The cockpit payload shall **carry** the three-state condition per optional field, and a view shall **read** that condition rather than derive it. Origin: `platform/T-0016` DoD 2–4 at its **third** touch, cut at the seam the ticket had named since sprint 16. The finding — made by a check built for SWR-135 — is that `cockpitKarte` formulated the rule *'null means no data, 0 means 0'* at **three** places inline (`team.letzter_digest`, `letzte_baseline`, the KPI pill), and ⚠ **all three were factually correct**, which is precisely the construction that cost SWR-131: four formulations of one concept, each right on its own, drifting apart unnoticed. ⚠ The ticket forbade writing code first and required the **contract question** to be answered first, because `/api/cockpit` carried no state field and a second derivation in JavaScript would have been a **new** B033 case rather than a repair. > **Solving a ticket against B033 by creating a new B033 case is not a solution but a deferral with better press.** The payload shall therefore gain a **sister key** `zustaende` — the same shape under which SWR-117 was allowed to add the org header block, so that every present reader of `eintrag['letzte_baseline']` keeps reading it unchanged; wrapping the values as `{wert, zustand}` (the dashboard's shape) would have changed **every** cockpit reader to deliver an annotation that concerns three fields. ⚠⚠ The set of keys shall follow the **contract** (`pflicht: false`) and not the present view: had it followed the view it would hold three entries, and the fourth (`letzte_baseline_text`) would have been missing for the first reader that displays it — a gap nobody notices, because it is visible only in the `null` case. ⚠ The migration shall be **without change of behaviour**: the three places display the right thing today, so the wordings shall be carried over character for character and a unification would be a behavioural change with a good conscience. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_cockpit_zustand.py`: (1) `zustaende` holds an entry for **every** `pflicht: false` field of the contract file — measured against the YAML, not against a list in the test; (2) `team.letzter_digest` is `nicht_geliefert` when `team` itself is, i.e. absent parent means absent child, not missing key; (3) the same value yields the same state in `cockpit` and in `dashboard`, which is the assurance that it is one derivation and not two) and JS tests (`regeln.test.cjs`: the nine wordings of three fields × three states, asserted character for character against the pre-migration strings, plus the counter test `test_altbestand_der_inline_regel_waechst_nicht` pulled to **0**) | medium | reviewed |

| SWR-147 | A team founding shall be **submitted, never performed**: the system shall produce a charter **draft** and a founding decision request in **one** commit, and the conditions that `steckbrief_pruefen` returns shall stand in the request's **prose**. Origin: `pm/T-0063`, part b of `pm/T-0028` from letter `pm/N-0022`, at its **fourth** touch — eight word-identical deferrals stand in its own history.
