**Der Preis, benannt.** Preflight wird ab hier **rot** für einen Zustand, der bisher nur eine
Zeile war. Das ist beabsichtigt und trotzdem ein Risiko: wer mitten in der Arbeit einen
Preflight laufen lässt, hat eine angefasste Anforderung auf der Platte und bekommt einen
Befund. Genau dafür gibt es `--skip-tests` als Vorbild — der Befund ist am Sprint**ende**
gemeint, wo `abschluss.cmd` ihn auswertet. Er blockiert dort den Push, und das ist die
richtige Stelle: ein Push mit einer ungeschriebenen Anforderung ist der Fehler, den er
verhindern soll.

| SWR-155 | The preflight shall report every task standing at `in_progress` while **no sprint is running** — with ref and title — as tasks that were started and left behind, and shall state the count **even when it is zero or when a sprint is running**. Origin: client letter `pm/N-0043` (2026-08-20), point 4: *„Wenn Sprint vorbei ist, muss ein anderer status drin stehen“*. ⚠⚠ The check only became meaningful with stage 1 of `pm/T-0069`: measured over the committed history, `in_progress` lived a **median of 22 seconds** and **159 of 300** closed tasks never held it at all — a check for a state that does not occur is permanently green and asserts nothing (`L-2026-08-17ai`). The order of the two stages is therefore not convenience. ⚠ **Report, never tidy up:** a script that resets the status makes the standstill invisible; the other status is a PM decision (done / deferred with reason / blocked) — the same downside `platform/T-0022` question 3 keeps open. ⚠ While a sprint runs the number is still printed and merely not counted as a finding: suppressing a result because it is currently harmless makes a check that ran indistinguishable from one that did not (SWR-114/SWR-117). The list is read from the **same** sprint view that counts `offen_gesamt` — no second collection path (B033). | STK-019 | `test_liegengeblieben.py` (9, every one **producing** the state instead of hoping for it: finding without a running sprint, no finding with one, `open`/`blocked`/`in_review` excluded, unreadable view is `None` not empty, one single view call, refs printed, zero still prints a line, running sprint counts but does not judge) | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-156 | The preflight shall state the **pause between the end of the previous sprint and the beginning of the current run**, in minutes and as a **multiple of the takt recorded in the register**, and shall report it as a finding beyond `STILLE_TAKTE` takte. Origin: client letter `team-mail/N-0004` (2026-08-20): *„in der konfiguration ist tägliches routine eigerichtet, jedoch wurde das nicht ausgeführt … im aktuellen sprint müsste das aufgefallen sein!?"* — measured, the pause between sprint 20 and 21 was **3 612 minutes at a 60-minute takt, sixty times the takt**, and no output of that run mentioned it. ⚠⚠ No existing check could have found it: `sprint_register.nicht_beendete()` (SWR-136) examines sprints **without `ende`**, i.e. runs that broke off mid-way — a check for a **trace**. *A run that fails leaves no trace and is the only one that cannot report itself*; what is measurable instead is the **distance** between two runs, a quantity that exists even when nothing happened in between. ⚠ The line shall appear **even when the pause is unremarkable** (SWR-114/117/155). ⚠ The threshold decision shall be taken by `session.stille()` — the **same** function the "Letzte Session" tile has used since SWR-102 — and the takt shall be read from the **register**, never from a constant in the source: measured in sprint 22, `session.TAKT_MINUTEN` held **30** while the register and `sprint_register.TAKT_MIN_STANDARD` held **60**, so tile and preflight would have declared silence at different times over the same fact (B033). ⚠⚠ A **negative** pause shall be reported as an **overlap** and never clamped to zero: of seven computable pauses in the register exactly **one** is negative (sprint 17 starts 16:49, sprint 16 ends 17:10) — it is the only evidence that the register's timestamps come from the clock of whichever run wrote them, and clamping would erase it. ⚠ Sprints without `ende` before the cutoff (SWR-136, **14** of them) yield no computable pause; the check shall **say so** instead of inventing one. | STK-019 | `test_pause_zwischen_laeufen.py` (14, every register **built** rather than hoped for: the real 3 612-minute outage red, the real 42-minute pause green, the threshold pinned at 119/120/121 minutes, the measured negative pause reported as overlap, sprints without `ende` named, first run and empty register unberechenbar-with-reason, takt from register not source, no second threshold comparison in the module, tile and preflight agree, preflight prints the line when unremarkable, as finding with number and period, and as "nicht prüfbar") | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-157 | An assertion against the **real** inventory shall assert what the code *does* — the **selection**, the recognition, the state — and shall **never** pin a snapshot of that inventory: no fixed date and no fixed count that normal work invalidates. Where a magnitude matters, a **bound** shall be asserted (`assertGreaterEqual`), which grows with the inventory instead of breaking against it. Origin: `platform/T-0024` — `test_widget_post.BestandTest` held the newest daily digest against the literal `2026-08-16`; a new digest appeared on 2026-08-17 at **22:22**, four minutes after the closing report of sprint 20, and the assertion stood **red for three days** unnoticed. ⚠⚠ The intent was right and is preserved (SWR-128: *mock-ups do not suffice*); the price was an assertion with an **expiry date** — *a test against the real inventory checks more than a mock, but once it pins a snapshot it checks the clock instead of the code from tomorrow on*. ⚠ The date shall **not** be incremented: that defers the finding to the next digest instead of removing it. ⚠ The **counting comes before the repair** (question 3 of the ticket, same obligation as `platform/T-0022` question 2): measured over all 66 test files by syntax tree — not by text search, which has produced five false alarms in this organisation — there is exactly **one** assertion of this construction, while the sound counter-construction (a bound over the real inventory) already existed in **two** places. A permanent check shall hold that count at zero and shall **produce** its own subject in a counter-check, since a count stuck at zero is otherwise indistinguishable from a broken count (`L-2026-08-17ai`). ⚠ The check shall state what it does **not** cover: it recognises an ISO date literal, not an arbitrary hardcoded number, and therefore asserts a lower bound rather than completeness. | STK-019 | `test_widget_post.py` (32 total; 6 new or rewritten: state/count/reaction as bounds instead of a snapshot, newest daily and weekly digest derived from the **directory**, `digest_liste` newest-first — the assumption `post_widget` documents in a comment and nothing asserted — plus the AST count at zero and its counter-check on a fabricated module) | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-158 | A generator that replaces a file shall **read its own predecessor before it replaces it**, and shall compare by **requirement IDs**, not by count. If an ID present in the existing target is absent from the new state, the run shall **write nothing**, shall **name** the missing IDs and shall exit non-zero. Origin: `platform/T-0020` — in sprint 17 `swr-test-matrix.md` lost 121 lines, the requirement count fell from **143** to **24**, the message read „Matrix geschrieben" and the exit code was **0**. ⚠⚠ The three questions that held this build back for four sprints were answered in sprint 22 by **measurement over all 95 commits** of the file, ID set per revision: of **94** transitions, **not one** ever lost an ID (span 21 → 157, monotonically growing). (1) *IDs, not counts* — an ID check would have produced **zero** false alarms over the whole history, a count check is blind at constant size. (2) *Shrinking is legitimate: for now, never* — the three imagined exceptions (a requirement is `rejected`, a product gets its own matrix, a project is archived) have **never occurred** at the canonical matrix, and a `rejected` requirement keeps its row anyway; therefore **no** `--darf-schrumpfen` flag: *a flag every caller sets is not a rule, and an exception that has never occurred is not an exception but a guess about the future*. This is expressly a decision **with an expiry date**: the first real case becomes a finding, and the exception is then built against a real example instead of an imagined one. (3) *`--produkt` too, but per target* — the comparison runs against the **target file** the call already knows, so a deliberately small product matrix measures itself against its own predecessor and the check never fails at its own exception. ⚠ **Nothing is written** — same construction as SWR-145 one level up: the sprint-17 damage lived and died in a working copy and never reached a commit, so a warning *after* the write would have reported it and still caused it. ⚠ A target that does not yet exist is **not** an empty one: a matrix that never existed cannot shrink. | STK-019 | `test_trace_matrix.py` (6 new: the sprint-17 case replayed and **rejected** with the old file byte-identical afterwards, the vanished ID named in the message, growth and constancy green as counter-check, first run without predecessor green, product matrix measured against its own predecessor while the canonical one stays untouched, and `bestand_ids` reading only the first table column) | medium | **reviewed** |

| SWR-159 | The register check shall assert an **impossibility, not a threshold**: a register line cannot have been written **later** than the commit that carries it. Every register timestamp (`start`, `ende`, `beobachtet`) shall be compared against the commit time of the commit that introduced the line, and a timestamp lying in the **future** of its own commit shall be reported as a finding, naming both times and the distance. The preflight shall print the line **even at zero hits** (SWR-114/117/155). Origin: `platform/T-0026` — one of seven computable pauses in the register is **negative**. ⚠⚠ The measurement **reversed the ticket's own suspicion**: measured over all 31 register events, the six regular `ende` events lie at **+0.6 to +1.1** minutes, the documented retro-fitted `ende` of sprint 17 at **+21.3**, and the `ende` of sprint **16** at **−37.4** — commit `911e57a` of 16:32:36 carries the line `"ende": "2026-08-17 17:10"`. *No process can be 38 minutes ahead of its own clock.* The wrong value is therefore the **end of sprint 16**, not the start of sprint 17 (16:49), which falls cleanly between the commits of 16:40:58 and 17:34:16. ⚠ Of the ticket's three hypotheses: *retro-fitted* is excluded (a retro-fit yields a **positive** distance, exactly as the documented one does), *timezone/DST* is excluded by magnitude (offsets are multiples of 15 minutes, a DST shift is exactly 60; 37.4 is neither), leaving *two runs, two clocks* — independently supported by two recorded concurrency findings of the same day. ⚠ **Which** of the two clocks was right shall not be claimed: the only two witnesses are the two disputing clocks, and no third exists in the inventory. ⚠ The register shall therefore **not** be converted to write an offset: both commits of the window carry `+02:00`, so a recorded offset would **not** have found this case — the dispute was in the minute hand, and a format change to an append-only file that misses the only documented disturbance is effort without yield (B038). ⚠ The check is **one-sided** by design: `start` distances scatter from +0.9 to +81.3 minutes because a start is written early and committed late, so any two-sided threshold would have to guess. Minute granularity cannot trigger it falsely — truncation makes a value **earlier**, never later. Over the whole history: exactly **one** hit, **zero** false alarms. | STK-019 | `test_uhrenprobe.py` (8, every register **built** rather than hoped for, commit times set via the environment: the documented case found and fully named, the normal case green, the retro-fit green as the reason for one-sidedness, minute truncation green, `beobachtet` lines included, missing register and missing history each `None` rather than „clean", plus a bound over the **real** inventory that keeps the single piece of evidence findable) | medium | **reviewed** |

| SWR-160 | The **content** of a widget shall lie behind the PIN read gate, while the **tile itself stays visible** without a PIN. The unprotected route (`GET /api/widgets`) shall carry the figures — date, mail count, number of open points — and shall declare, per widget, **that** a content exists and **where** it lies (`inhalt_gesperrt`, `inhalt_route`); it shall never carry the content itself. The content route shall live **inside** the existing `/api/team` branch and shall inherit its PIN check rather than bring its own. Origin: `projects/p11/T-0013`, split from `p11/T-0009` in sprint 17 because **an access decision is not a layout** (B025) — deferred three times for exactly that reason and built at the fourth touch. ⚠⚠ The distinction that carries the whole build: *the **number** of open points is a metric; their **wording** is subject lines, senders and links.* `reaktionspunkte` counts, `reaktionspunkte_text` quotes — the same rubric, the same cut, two entirely different disclosures. Two selection rules over one rubric would be B033, and the tile would then count other points than the gate shows. ⚠ The tile shall **not disappear** without a PIN: *a tile that vanishes discloses nothing and claims in doing so that there is nothing here* — the same confusion as „no data" versus „0" (SWR-108/135), one storey up. ⚠⚠ And the lock shall **not** become a fourth state. `wert`/`echte_null`/`nicht_geliefert` describe **what** the source delivers; a lock describes **who** may see it. `inhalt_gesperrt` is therefore a sibling key, not a value — widget contract **v2.7**, a sub-version: no reader changes. ⚠ Without release **nothing** shall pass, not „less": a gate that serves an abridged version is a leak with a clear conscience. The check shall sit at the **endpoint**, not in the view — asserted over the **syntax tree**, counting calls to `widgets.widget_inhalt` outside the PIN branch (**0**), and the count shall find its own subject so that a zero cannot be mistaken for a broken check. | STK-019 | `test_widget_pin_gate.py` (10: number and wording from the same selection, missing rubric `None` rather than `[]`, exactly one PIN branch and no call outside it, the unprotected route never touching the content, tile visible and self-declaring, no fourth state, nothing of the **real** digest in the PIN-free payload, content present behind the gate, team without `widget.yaml` `None` rather than an empty shell, unknown takt no silent fallback to the newest) | medium | **reviewed** |

| SWR-161 | Mandatory artefacts that only the **founding path** creates shall be checked **per repository** and **reported**, never healed in passing. The preflight shall print the line even at zero findings. Origin: `platform/T-0022` questions 2 and 3. ⚠⚠ The obligation was *count before you repair*, and the count shrank the suspicion: of the **six** artefacts `pool._projekt_dateien_schreiben` creates, measured over all **17** discovered projects and teams, exactly **one** was missing in more than one place and **four** were missing nowhere. *A repair per site would have been the same assumption once more, and a check over all six a permanent finding about five correct cases.* ⚠⚠ The answer to question 3 is therefore **both, with a division of labour**: SWR-152 creates the decision log when a decision is booked — right, because *a decision that fails at its filing place is lost the moment the window closes* — but it heals only where somebody walked into it. `promt-team` has had its log since the incident of 2026-08-17; `platform` had none until sprint 23. **The self-healing path cannot find the defect nobody has walked into yet; only a check that walks every repository can.** The check shall therefore **read and report**, and shall create nothing. ⚠ `docs/01-projektauftrag.md` shall **not** be in the list although it is missing in 6 of 17: five of those keep `01-team-charter.md`, `01-rollenbeschreibung.md` or `02-initialprojekt-p0.md` instead, so a check would have to **guess** which name applies (B038) — and a finding pointing at five correct cases trains looking away (SWR-109/110/112). The one real case (`platform` has neither) is a question for the PM, not a check. ⚠ The check sees only what the discovery sees: `produkt-datakonv` also has no decision log and is **not** among the 17 — widening the discovery is a separate decision and is not taken here. | STK-019 | `test_pflichtartefakte.py` (5: the real inventory complete, the check **producing its own subject** on a fabricated repo, the division of labour held — the check creates nothing —, the head of the newly created log **imported** from `pool.LOG_TABELLENKOPF` rather than copied, and the shortness of the list frozen so that growth is a decision) | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-162 | The transition check shall examine **every** discovered project, including those inside a collection repository, and shall recognise a ticket file at **any depth**. Where a project has no `.git` of its own, the enclosing repository shall be checked — searching upwards to the given root and **no further**, so that the tool never claims jurisdiction over foreign history. Origin: sprint 23, found because this very run made a booking error and went looking for why nothing turned red. ⚠⚠ `uebergang_historie` was blind to `projects` (p10, p11, p12) on **two** paths at once, each harmless-looking on its own: `pruefe_alle` skipped every project without its own `.git` — **while the comment beside it said that then the collection repo counts** — and `status_wechsel` filtered `git log -- tickets/` **relative to the repo root**, which matches nothing one level down. **66 status changes went unexamined since SWR-118 (sprint 9)**; hidden in them, four old violations and one new. > **A comment describing what the code should do is not an assertion. Here it asserted the opposite of what stood beside it for three sprints, and nobody compared the two.** ⚠ `ALTBESTAND_ERWARTET` rises **52 → 56** — not because history was rewritten but because four old cases became **visible**. The cutoff shall **not** be moved: that is exactly the silent parking the frozen number exists to prevent. ⚠ The fifth case lies **after** the cutoff and is this run's own: `projects/p11/T-0009.md` went `in_progress → done` in one commit. It is **named and not smoothed**, and it is the fourth standing finding. ⚠ The path filter shall carry an explicit **depth** guarantee (`:(glob)**/tickets/*`); a bare `*/tickets/` is a prefix and would go blind again one level deeper. ⚠ Dropping the filter entirely was the first draft and was **measured to be worse**, not better: 0.4–2.9 s more per repo. The draft's comment claimed „about 2 s" — estimated, not measured, and the sixth instance of exactly the family `platform/T-0027` was opened for. | STK-019 | `test_uebergang_sammelrepo.py` (6: a violation one level deeper found on a **built** collection repo, `_repo_wurzel` finding the enclosing repo, the upward search stopping at the root, only ticket files counting, plus two bounds over the real inventory — the collection repo examined at all, and the frozen old-stock number matching) | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-163 | The one write path to Git shall **clear the lock its own successful call leaves behind**, immediately after that call returns. Origin: `platform/T-0021` at its **fourth** touch, built after three deferrals. The finding is a **measurement** from sprint 21 and it **reverses** the assumption in the ticket's own head: the artefacts under `objects/**/tmp_obj_*` are **litter, not a lock** — a commit passed with five `warning: unable to unlink` and exit **0** — while the lock that actually stops the next call is `.git/index.lock`. ⚠⚠ And the call that leaves it is the one that **succeeds**: `git status --porcelain` returns exit 0 and leaves the lock, whereas `git add`, `git commit`, `git log`, `git diff` and `git ls-files` leave nothing. > **Renaming is permitted on this mount, deleting is not. Git ends a WRITING index operation by renaming `index.lock` over `index`, which passes; it ends a merely READING refresh by DELETING the lock, which fails. The harmless read leaves behind the lock the next call dies on.** The fallback built in SWR-134 therefore does not cover this case at all: it repairs the call that **failed**, and so shifts the cost onto the **next** caller, who sees an error that looks like a foreign process. ⚠ The clearing shall happen **after** the own call and **never before** the first Git call: before it, nothing is known about a lock found lying there, and moving aside one held by a *running* process is worse than leaving it (`platform/T-0015` DoD 2, unchanged in force). The second question — *is anyone else running?* — shall be asked with the organisation's **existing** mechanism (`preflight.git_prozess_aktiv`) and not a second one, and on any error it shall decide in the same direction that mechanism does: touch nothing. ⚠⚠ The clearing shall call **no git at all** — the requirement inherited from SWR-159, where the first draft of the clock check called git inside the sprint counter and was stopped by an assurance from sprint 16 (`test_die_messung_ruft_KEIN_git_auf`, SWR-134): *a tool that works on concurrency and creates a lock while doing so is its own damage case*. The count shall live in its **own** field (`nachgeraeumt`), not fold into `geraeumt`: *how much had to go so that I got through* and *how much did I leave for the next one* are two questions, and one field could answer only one of them. A failing clean-up shall **never** turn a successful commit into a failure. | STK-021 | Unit tests (`test_git_schreibweg.py::NachraeumenTest`: after a successful commit the clean-up runs and its count lands in `nachgeraeumt`; a **reading** `ruf` clears behind itself too, with `geraeumt` staying 0 — the pair that separates the two questions; ⚠ **counter-check** that after a **failure** nothing is cleared afterwards and the clean-up runs **exactly once**, or the assurance *once, not in a loop* would silently have become *twice*; ⚠ counter-check that a **running git** stops the clean-up entirely; ⚠⚠ counter-check that `_nachraeumen` starts **no git process**, asserted by observing `subprocess.run`; a throwing clean-up leaves the successful commit successful; both counters exist as separate slots — and `test_ohne_pfade_gibt_es_kein_zwischenraeumen` **sharpened** from *no clearing at all* to *no clearing before the first git call*, the same move sprint 16 made on its neighbour) | high | reviewed |

| SWR-164 | The preflight shall **measure and report** how many cleared locks lie parked in `.git/verwaiste-locks`, across all repositories and naming the largest single holding, and shall print the line **even when nothing is remarkable** (SWR-114/117/155). Origin: `platform/T-0021` question 3 — *does the parking lot grow without bound?* — and the measured answer is **yes**, for a reason that sits inside the clearing mechanism itself: `entferne_artefakte` tries `os.remove` first and **renames** into the parking lot on `OSError`, so on a mount without `unlink` rights the first stage fails **always** and every cleared lock is parked and never deleted. Measured: 1975 files in `pm` in sprint 21, **2099** in sprint 24, **9336** across all repositories. ⚠ The line shall be **no finding** and shall block nothing: what is missing is the delete permission, not an idea, and a standing warning about a condition nobody can end trains people to look away — the same reasoning that froze the 42 legacy DRs in SWR-131. ⚠ The measurement shall **clear nothing**: a measurement that changes its subject measures itself, and emptying the lot while counting would make the growth question permanently unanswerable. ⚠ Where no parking lot exists at all the answer shall be **"not measurable"** and not **0** — *not measured* and *measured as zero* look alike as a number and are two different facts (SWR-114, and the equality that hid "zero JS tests" for five sprints in SWR-128). The largest holding shall be named **by repository**, since a person who wants to empty it on the host needs the repository and not the sum (SWR-110: name, do not count). | STK-021 | Unit tests (`test_parkplatz_stand.py`: no repository at all yields `None`, not 0 — the counter-check without which the number would be worthless; counting runs across all repositories; the largest single holding is named by repository; ⚠ the measurement leaves the lot untouched, asserted by counting the files again afterwards; and on the **real holding** the total is at least three digits, asserted as an **order of magnitude** rather than a fixed number, because a test that nails today's count is red tomorrow and says nothing — the mistake behind SWR-157) | medium | reviewed |

| SWR-165 | A decision that is **recorded in the log** and **invisible in its ticket** shall be reported as a finding, and the line shall be printed even at zero. Origin: `platform/T-0022` question 1 at its **fourth** touch. The three writes of `inbox.entscheide` were **counted before anything was rebuilt**: (1) append to `management/decisions/decision-log.md`, (2) append the body marker to `tickets/T-xxxx.md`, (3) overwrite `BOARD.md`, then commit — and only the first is guarded against its own absence (SWR-152). The failure of 2026-08-17 hit the **first**, which is why it was clean: *luck in the ordering, not an assurance.* ⚠⚠ The dangerous seam is between 1 and 2, and it is the **worse direction**: `board.dr_entschieden` reads *decided* from the **body marker**, so a write that dies after the log leaves the decision recorded while **every** check still considers the DR open — and the client is handed a question he has already answered. That is literally the incident behind SWR-131 with the roles swapped: there the marker was present and the status missing. > **A decision that is minuted and invisible in the ticket is worse than one that never arrived: the second one the human notices, the first one he does not.** ⚠ The build shall be a **check beside the path**, not a rebuild of the path: altering the write path of a Klasse-A decision demands a ruling on what holds when the second of three files fails — rollback, partial state, or refusal before the first write — and that question has been put to nobody. Answering it unasked is the mistake `platform/T-0020` avoided for four sprints. ⚠ Only lines the inbox wrote shall be checked, recognised by a last column that is **exactly** a ticket ID; hand-written entries carry an artefact list there, and demanding a marker of them would start the check **47 times red** on its first day — the lesson of the 42 legacy DRs in SWR-131. The marker itself shall live at **one** place — `board.ENTSCHEIDUNGSMARKER`, where it has sat since SWR-039/131: two spellings of one marker would be a check that eventually finds nothing and stays green. ⚠⚠ **The first draft of this very requirement created a second one** (`aggregation.DR_RUMPFMARKER`) and was made red not by its author but by `test_dr_verbuchung.test_keine_zweite_kopie_des_markers_im_quelltext`, an assurance from sprint 17 that counts how many files carry the marker literal — *a requirement demanding 'the marker lives at one place' whose first draft added a second.* That is the **second run in a row** in which an older assurance discarded this house's own draft (SWR-159 in sprint 23), and it is the argument for keeping such counting tests even when they look pedantic. A missing ticket **file** shall be its own reason, not an exception. Measured at build time (sprint 24): **93** log lines across all repositories, **46** of them written by the inbox, **0** without a marker. | STK-021 | Unit tests (`test_decision_log_marker.py`: a log line whose ticket lacks the marker is found, named with `D`-ID and ticket; ⚠ counter-check that a line **with** marker is silent, without which the check would be green even if it read nothing; ⚠⚠ counter-check that **hand-written** lines are not demanded of; a missing ticket file yields its own reason instead of an exception; the marker `inbox.entscheide` writes is asserted to **be** `board.ENTSCHEIDUNGSMARKER`, read from `inbox.py`, while the **count** of copies stays where it already lives — repeating it here would be the same duplication one level up; and on the **real holding** the answer is 0 **while the base set is provably non-empty** — a green check over an empty set says nothing, the SWR-128 lesson) | high | reviewed |

| SWR-166 | The preflight shall count **two** kinds of finding and let only the first decide its exit code: **blocking** — something the caller can act on now (uncommitted files, unbooked status, missing mandatory artefacts, red tests, a status jump made **in the running sprint**) — and **carried forward** — a fact frozen before the running sprint began and repairable by nobody (a status jump committed in a **closed** sprint, since history is not rewritten here; a pause that has already elapsed). Carried-forward findings shall appear **unchanged in wording, by name and with commit**, and the closing line shall state **both** numbers even when one is zero. Origin: `platform/T-0029`, third contribution to letter `team-mail/N-0004`. ⚠⚠ The cost is **measured, not argued**: the auto-push watchdog last succeeded on **2026-08-17 11:32:30** and has since aborted **83 times in a row** over three days, pushing nothing; the Ollama fast-tick the client had himself registered ran **6 times** and aborted **all 12** of its ticks — both because `preflight` returns exit 1 while any finding stands, and four of the standing findings are status transitions committed in closed sprints that nobody can undo. > **A guard that blocks on a fact nobody can change any more is not a guard but a switch somebody flipped and nobody noticed.** ⚠⚠ The rule against this **already existed in the same module and is applied twice** — to the 56 pre-cutoff cases (*„reported and does not block — not smoothed over, but not a standing finding that trains looking away"*) and to the clock check's single legacy row (*„the file is append-only, it is not repairable"*). At the third site it was simply not applied, and there it cost three days of operation. **This is B033 with the copy being a justification rather than a code site.** ⚠ The cutoff shall **not** be moved (`ALTBESTAND_ERWARTET` exists precisely to make that visible) and **no register of individual acknowledged cases** shall be created (the module head argues why). A **second boundary** is derived from the same number the first one uses — the commit time — and taken from the sprint register: the start of the running sprint, or the end of the last one. ⚠ Where that boundary cannot be determined the old behaviour shall hold — **everything after the cutoff blocks** — deciding in the direction this house always decides under uncertainty: let nothing through. ⚠⚠ The boundary function shall call **no git** (the obligation inherited from SWR-159/SWR-134: a tool working on concurrency that creates a lock while doing so is its own damage case). ⚠ **Honest limit:** the register-overlap finding and a *new* clock-check row are the same category and are **not** converted here — both currently measure zero, and converting a check that has never fired could not be verified against the real holding. Named rather than done. | STK-021 | Unit tests (`test_uebergang_historie.LaufgrenzeTest`, the **pair** per `L-2026-08-20by`: a violation from a closed sprint no longer blocks — **and** a violation in the running sprint still does, without which the change would be an abolition; the cutoff takes precedence over the new boundary, so that `ALTBESTAND_ERWARTET` keeps its force; `laufgrenze` starts **no** subprocess, asserted by observing `subprocess.run`; over the **real holding**: the running sprint is clean, the four carried-forward cases are **present and each names repo, ticket file and commit** — without that second half a pass that reports nothing would also be green —, and the sum of both buckets is conserved) | high | **reviewed** |
| SWR-167 | The orchestrator tick shall close with a message that **names the outcome the gateway reported**: „abgeschlossen" only where an artefact was delivered, otherwise the gateway's status and its message. Origin: `platform/T-0031`, the **first three ticks that ever ran through** after SWR-166 unlocked the preflight. ⚠⚠ The cost is **measured, not argued**: all three (20:00 `platform`, 20:15 `platform`, 20:15 `team-mail`) printed `Gateway: status=fehler provider= kosten=0.00 € artefakte=[]` and, on the next line, `Tick abgeschlossen. Review/PR: Branch feature/...`. The `print` stood **unconditionally** before `return 0`, after the success, waiting and error branches alike. > **Two lines above one another that contradict each other, and the lower one is the one that looks like a result. „Abgeschlossen" is not a wrong word for a failure — it is not a word about the result at all, but about the end of the function.** ⚠ Sprint 25 had written down, in its own report, that it had **not** measured whether a tick does anything useful after the unlock, *„so that it is not later taken as done because nobody contradicted it"*. This is that measurement, and the answer is no. ⚠ The assertion shall be a **pair**: the success wording must still appear for a delivering tick, otherwise a version that never says „abgeschlossen" would pass every test that only checks the failure case. | STK-021 | Unit tests (`test_tick_ergebnismeldung`: gateway `fehler` → the closing line carries the status and the gateway message and **not** the word „abgeschlossen"; gateway `ok` **with** artefact → it does carry it; `wartet` → neither; the run registry entry and the closing line name the **same** status, so that log and registry cannot drift apart) | high | **reviewed** |
| SWR-168 | The tick shall return to the base branch **before** writing ticket and board, and shall **verify that it did**; where the return fails it shall abort with a finding instead of continuing to write on the feature branch. A branch left over from an **earlier** tick of the same ticket shall not cause HEAD to be moved **backwards** onto that older tip. Origin: `platform/T-0031`. ⚠⚠ Measured from the `platform` reflog of 2026-08-20: the branch name is built from ticket id and title and is therefore **the same at every tick**; `T-0001` is a standing order (`takt: je-session`), so the second tick is not the exception but the rule, and at a 15-minute cadence it arrives after 15 minutes. `checkout -b` failed, the fallback `checkout <branch>` put HEAD on the tip from 20:00 — **two commits back** — and the result commit landed there. `main` and the branch have **diverged** (merge base a370306, main +2, branch +1). ⚠⚠ The consequence is the part that matters: `main` kept `status: in_progress` while the working tree showed `open`, and the preflight — which reads the **working tree** — reported *„In Arbeit liegengeblieben: 0"* for a ticket that had been `in_progress` on `main` since 20:15. > **A check that reads the working tree checks whichever branch it happens to be standing on. As long as nobody switches branches those are the same thing, and that is exactly why it goes unnoticed the one time they are not.** ⚠ The run registry entry of the 20:15 run existed **only** on the branch — with `logging.run_registry: required` in the guardrails that is a lost record; it was carried over to `main` in commit `e532681`. ⚠ What was already built is the precondition **before** the tick (`p0/T-0014`, clean working copy) and it held. The condition **after** the tick — *is HEAD back where it was?* — was never built: **the care was all at the start of the run; what a run leaves behind, nobody checks.** ⚠ `fehler_ok=True` on the return shall be removed, not kept alongside. | STK-021 | Unit tests (`test_tick_branchrueckkehr`: an existing branch from an earlier tick does not move HEAD backwards — asserted on the recorded git calls; a failing return aborts and the ticket/board write does **not** happen; the successful case still returns to the base branch, without which an abort-always version would pass; over the **real** finding: with `main` and branch diverged the result commit does not land on the branch) | high | **reviewed** |
| SWR-169 | The gateway shall take the Ollama model from the **role's entry in the staffing register** where one is set; `OLLAMA_MODEL` shall remain the override for the single case and the guardrails value the fallback. The order shall be the same in the code and in the docstring. Origin: `platform/T-0032`. ⚠⚠ All three ticks of 2026-08-20 died at the same place: `ollama: Anfrage fehlgeschlagen (404): {"error":"model 'llama3.1:8b' not found"}`. ⚠⚠ **And that exact finding has stood in the holding as a lesson since 2026-08-06** — `L-003 (T-0011/F13)`: *„the guardrails default `llama3.1:8b` was **not installed** on the team node (present: `gemma3:27b`) … **check model defaults against the device register; follow up deviations as a registry/guardrails CR**"* — written down a second time in `p0/management/sprint-1/retro.md` and a third time in `p0/tickets/T-0016.md`, there with a number beside it: **„expected repeat rate of these failure patterns in sprint 2 = 0"**. In sprint 26 the rate is **3 of 3**. > **The lesson was written down, three times, in three places, with an expected value. The sentence that carried its execution — „follow up as a registry/guardrails CR" — never became a ticket. Fourteen days and twenty-five sprints later the same model name stops the first three real ticks.** ⚠ Two places carry the model and the gateway reads the wrong one: `guardrails.yaml` says `llama3.1:8b`, while `organigramm.json` carries `gemma3:27b` for both `takt: schnell` roles (PROB, MAIL-RED) and `pm/D010` names the same — **B033, with a configuration value as the forgotten copy rather than a code site**. ⚠ The direction of the fix follows from **where the client maintains the value**: the register he edits in the HMI, so that a wrong entry is correctable in one visible place. A second default in code would only be a third copy. ⚠ **Honest limit, with the place of measurement stated (`L-2026-08-20ce`):** whether `gemma3:27b` is installed on the client's machine is **not** measurable from the Cowork sandbox and is not asserted here. | STK-021 | Unit tests (`test_modellaufloesung`: register value wins over the guardrails default; `OLLAMA_MODEL` wins over the register; empty register entry falls back to guardrails; **no** Ollama call in the test — the resolution is asserted, not the reachability, since a test that needed a running Ollama would be permanently red in this sandbox and would train exactly the looking-away SWR-166 was built against) | high | **reviewed** |
| SWR-170 | A check shall report every Ollama-staffed role whose model in the staffing register **deviates** from the guardrails default, and shall print the line **even at zero** (SWR-114/117/155). Origin: `platform/T-0032`, the counterpart without which SWR-169 would be a rule with no representative. ⚠ Expected at introduction: **2** (PROB, MAIL-RED) — the check is therefore **not empty on its first day**, and that is its own counter-check against the empty-ground-set error of SWR-128/SWR-165. > **A rule that no check represents does not last three sprints.** This one lasted fourteen days, because it had none. ⚠ The check reports the deviation and does **not** heal it: which of the two values is right is a question for the person who maintains the register, not for a tool. | STK-021 | Unit tests (`test_modellabweichung`: with the real holding the line names **2** roles by name; equal values → line present and zero; role without register model → not counted, since an unset value is not a deviation; the ground set of Ollama-staffed roles is asserted to be ≥ 1, without which a check reading nothing would also be green) | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-171 | Where the provider is **forced from outside** (`--provider`), the tick shall check **before the gateway call, before the branch and before any status change** that the drawn role actually **has a staffing entry with the matching motor in that unit**, and shall otherwise stop without a call, naming role, unit, missing motor and the instances that *are* staffed that way. Origin: `platform/T-0033`, from the 21:30 run of 2026-08-20 that sprint 26 had not planned. ⚠⚠ The finding is about **counter-checks, not about a model name**: `SWR-169` resolves the Ollama model from the staffing register and is backed by **four** counter-checks — and it had **zero effect in operation**, because all four asserted the resolving function and **none its caller**. `pm/D010` decided the fast cadence per **occupation** (`platform/PROB`, `team-mail/MAIL-RED`); `ollama-schnelltakt.cmd` passes only the **unit**, and `waehle_ticket` therefore draws the next ticket of **any** active AI role — drawn were `CM@platform` and `DEV@team-mail`. > **A counter-check that asserts the function and not its caller measures the half you wrote yourself. The requirement was green, the effect was nil, and without the accidental 21:30 run this sprint would have reported „SWR-169 built" and got away with it.** ⚠ What is asserted is the **occupation**, not the model: `DEV@team-mail` **is not in the staffing register at all** — the tick handed work to an instance nobody staffs, and the empty model name was the consequence, not the cause. A check on the model would go quiet the moment someone fills in a model; this one does not. ⚠ The mapping provider→motor is deliberately **partial and named** (`ollama` only): `claude`/`copilot` are called `cowork` in the staffing register, and whether that means a Claude call or a human at the keyboard is written down nowhere. An invented row would be the semantics that `platform/T-0033` rejected as option 2. ⚠ Without `--provider` the check does not apply — then the role's provider chain from `registry.yaml` governs, and the staffing register has nothing to say about it; a check that applied here too would stop this house's own session run. ⚠ Exit code **0**, not 1: the run is not broken, there is nothing to do for this occupation — the same category as „no workable ticket". The **wording** carries the reason, which is what SWR-167 demands. ⚠ **Honest limit, with the place of measurement stated (`L-2026-08-20ce`):** whether `gemma3:27b` is installed on the client's machine remains unmeasurable from the Cowork sandbox and is not asserted. | STK-021 | Unit tests (`test_besetzung_am_aufrufer`: a role without an Ollama occupation stops **before** `gateway.execute` — asserted with a spy on the real `tick()` over a real ticket holding, not on the helper — and the ticket file is **byte-identical** afterwards; the **pair**: a role *with* the occupation reaches the gateway and does so with a **non-empty** `modell_name`, without which an always-aborting version would pass every assertion above; `--provider` absent → check inactive; against the **real** holding the two drawn instances `CM@platform` and `DEV@team-mail` are named and resolve to no occupation, while `PROB@aspice` and `MAIL-RED@team-mail` do; ground set of Ollama occupations asserted ≥ 1) | high | **reviewed** |
| SWR-172 | The tick shall be restrictable to **one role** (`--rolle`), so that what `pm/D010` decided per occupation is **expressible at the call**. Origin: `platform/T-0033`. Until now only the unit could be passed, and that is where the role was lost between decision and call. ⚠⚠ **The switch is built and deliberately not thrown.** `ollama-schnelltakt.cmd` stays unchanged: it is the client's automation, running every 15 minutes, and the consequence was **counted before building** — of **14** open tickets across all repositories on 2026-08-20, **0** carry a role with an Ollama occupation. Restricting the cadence today would therefore not be a fix but a **shutdown**, and that is his decision, not ours (`platform/T-0035`, class C but his machine, deadline 2026-08-27, default A). > **A change that a single run cannot carry all the way does not belong at that run's end.** | STK-021 | Unit tests (`test_besetzung_am_aufrufer.RollenFilterTest`: without the filter priority decides, with it the staffed role is drawn, a filter without a match returns **nothing** rather than the next best ticket — the very error this ticket exists for; plus an assertion that `ollama-schnelltakt.cmd` does **not** carry `--rolle`, so that „built, not thrown" cannot quietly become „thrown") | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-173 | The measurable key figures of the closing report shall be **produced by one tool from their sources** — tests in the collection, test files, requirements, gaps, open letters, open tickets, decisions awaiting the client — and shall be carried in the sprint plan as a machine-readable block with the **time of measurement**. Origin: `platform/T-0027` at its **fourth** touch, built after three deferrals. ⚠⚠ The finding is **not** an arithmetic error. In five of six sprints the draft carried a **carried-forward** figure instead of a measured one; twice the gap was quantified (1155/1128, 1128/1147). All five were caught before the commit — **all five by recounting, not one by an assertion.** > **Each of those five corrections proves the care was there. What is missing is not attention but a place where the number COMES INTO BEING instead of being copied.** ⚠ **Script, not template** (question 2 of the ticket): the eighth instance stood in **prose** that no template governs — `9` instead of `11` JS assertions, under a heading reading *„counted, not overlooked"*. What found it was a pass over the file. ⚠ **Counted, not summed:** the figure is taken from the unittest **collection**, never from adding up test runs. Their divergence is what exposed the sprint-24 error (1201 vs 1208), where the batches followed a file list that changed **during** the run. One source, not two. ⚠ A **load error** shall count as a finding in its own right: a test file that fails to import simply does not appear in the collection, so a broken import would be reported as „fewer tests" — a wrong number with a correct provenance. | STK-021 | Unit tests (`test_berichtskennzahlen.MessungTest`: ground set asserted with orders of magnitude, so a version measuring 0 cannot pass; `ladefehler == 0`; gaps read from the generated matrix; those awaiting a human are a subset of the open tickets, so the two figures cannot count different sets) | high | **reviewed** |
| SWR-174 | An assertion shall hold the figures **written in the report** against a **fresh measurement** and shall fail on any divergence, including a **missing** figure. The parking lot of orphaned locks shall be **exempt from the equality check** and shall instead be required to carry a **time of measurement**. Origin: `platform/T-0027`, question 3 — *does the check run before or after the point at which the error does damage?* It runs as a unit test, therefore before the push. ⚠⚠ **Why the parking lot is treated differently from everything else here:** sprint 24 reported `9506` and marked it as a snapshot; sprint 25 measured `9754`. The figure was already wrong at reading time — not because anyone estimated, but because it stood there **without its timestamp**. > **A measured figure without the time of its measurement ages exactly as silently as an estimated one. The difference between the two is smaller than this house assumed.** ⚠ Putting it under equality instead would make the assertion red within minutes and would train precisely the looking-away that SWR-166 was built against — so the exemption is itself asserted, with its reason, rather than left to whoever edits the list next. ⚠ A **missing** figure counts as a divergence, without which a report carrying **no** numbers would be the greenest of all — the SWR-128 error applied to the report instead of the test set. ⚠ Expected value **written down before building** (the shape that proved itself in SWR-170): at first run the block is present and congruent — **0 divergences**. | STK-021 | Unit tests (`test_berichtskennzahlen.VergleichTest`/`SprintplanTest`: equal figures → no divergence; the real sprint-21 case 1155 vs 1128 → exactly one, named; empty report → one divergence per compared field; parking lot 9506 vs 10395 → **no** divergence, and `parkplatz` asserted absent from the comparison list; the real `pm/management/sprint-aktuell.md` carries the block, carries a timestamp, and matches the measurement) | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-175 | A unit that is **not a team** shall be able to carry a **display name** in its `steckbrief.yaml`; the order shall be team registry > profile > folder name, and the **discovery key shall stay the folder name**. Origin: `p9/T-0008`, decision `p9/D003` (option **A** plus the instruction *„rename P9 to Org-Cockpit"*), from the client's letter `p9/N-0001`: *„can this project be closed? why does it still exist?"* ⚠⚠ What the letter uncovered is not a naming issue: `p9` has **7 of 7** tickets `done` and nevertheless **78 commits in seven days**, because the requirements of the entire platform live in its requirements folder — **81 SWRs, nine of the last twenty-five from `platform`**. > **No check in this house asks whether the name above a folder is still true. A repository with `status: aktiv`, seven closed tickets and 78 commits in a week is invisible to every assertion we own. The client found it.** ⚠ The client chose **A** — nothing moves — and that choice is what makes the display name the right instrument: **an identity is not a label**. `p9` remains the key under which tickets, commits, staffing entries and cross-references stand; only what a human reads changes. Renaming the folder would have broken every `p9/requirements/...` reference in the holding for a cosmetic gain. ⚠ The team registry wins over the profile, because for teams it is the source of truth (header comment of the registry); letting the profile decide there would be two sources for one name — B033. | STK-021 | Unit tests (`test_anzeigename_einheit`: profile name beats the folder name; **absent** name falls back to the folder name, without which an always-renaming version would pass; a **blank** name is not a name; the team registry beats the profile; the discovery key stays the folder name — the second half of the pair and the actual meaning of option A; against the **real** holding `p9` reads „Org-Cockpit" while `p0`/`p1`/`p8` are untouched, over a ground set of more than ten units) | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-176 | Where a plan line names a **repository**, the drift check shall resolve **only** the qualified reference; the bare ticket id shall remain the fallback for hand-written lines that omit the repository and shall never override an explicitly named one. Origin: Sprint 27, found by **the preflight of this very run**. ⚠⚠ The check reported a **real** divergence between two things that have nothing to do with each other. The plan line `p9/T-0008` belongs to a **closed** ticket and therefore is not in `offene`; resolution fell back to the bare `T-0008`, which among the **open** tickets was unique — `promt-team/T-0008`, a different repository on a different sprint. > **Uniqueness was checked over the OPEN tickets while the line belonged to a CLOSED one. An id does not become unique because the remaining set is small.** ⚠ The sibling `statusdrift` resolves over **all** tickets and never fell into this — the same question with two ground sets, and only one of them chosen right. That is the SWR-128 family once more, this time in the **denominator** of a lookup rather than in a test set. | STK-021 | Unit tests (`test_preflight_plandrift.QualifizierteRefGewinntTest`: the real case — a closed `p9/T-0008` line does not drag in the open `promt-team/T-0008`; the **pair** — the ticket's own line is still checked, without which a version resolving nothing would pass; a line **without** a repository still resolves via the bare id, which is why the fallback exists; against the real plan the table is non-empty, so „0 drift" cannot mean „read nothing") | high | **reviewed** |

A second staffing of the same role in the same unit shall be answered with **409** naming pm/D012. | SWR-178 | There shall be exactly **one resolver** for effective staffing: every **active** project (project registry `typ aspice|projekt` or discovery project with steckbrief status `aktiv`) implicitly carries all core-team roles from the `core_team` block; explicit entries override the expansion on the same instance key; the PM team (typ `pm`) carries **no** core team. | SWR-179 | An implicit core-team instance shall be **detail-capable and configurable** in the HMI: saving it shall **materialize** an explicit deviation block (core defaults merged with the edited fields, marked as deviation); deleting it shall be refused with **400** and the instruction to set `status: pausiert` instead — there is no line one could delete, and a silent removal of a core role would contradict the project model. | SWR-180 | `projekt_setup.py` shall scaffold a new project **completely and without granting anything**: folder under `projects/<key>`, steckbrief (with display name, SWR-175), project order stub carrying the literal marker **„G0 OFFEN"**, project plan from the template, history, roles/ folder, and **12 board-valid tickets** — planning first (`prio: kritisch`), nine role initializations `blocked_by` the planning ticket, two recurring `je-session` tickets (PL monitoring, QM sample). It shall abort without writing anything if the target exists or the key is invalid. Origin: `pm/T-0073`; the button founds, it never decides (lesson p12/T-0001). | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_projekt_setup`: structure list, placeholder substitution, „G0 OFFEN" present, blocked_by chain, 12 tickets, `board.validiere_alle` empty, abort cases) | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-181 | Every project's CM plan (`docs/cm-plan.md`) shall carry its work-product list in **one machine-readable fenced block** (```` ```yaml work-products ````): per entry `pfad`, `name`, `eigentuemer` (role), optional `pruefstatus`. The block is the **single source** the work-product view reads — a second, prose-only list of the same facts would be B033. | SWR-182 | The HMI shall show a **work-product view** per unit: declared work products with existence, last change (git, read-only calls only — no call that leaves an index.lock on this mount, SWR-163), owner and check status, plus **both gap directions** (declared but missing; present under docs/ or verification/ but undeclared). Units without a CM plan shall show visibly „keine Daten" instead of an empty healthy state (SWR-096 pattern). Origin: `platform/T-0039`. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_workproducts`: gap pair; keine-Daten case) plus server smoke on the real holding (≥2 units with real work products) | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-183 | The HMI shall show one **communication timeline across all units**: letters (mailboxes) and decision requests (open and decided) merged chronologically, filterable by unit, newest first, with a hard entry cap per response (the view must not load everything). v1 explicitly excludes ticket history remarks — they live in ticket bodies without a structured timestamp; claiming them would be a promise the data cannot keep. | SWR-184 | Entries of units whose data class is **sensibel** shall appear in the communication timeline **only** when the existing PIN read gate passes (same rule and same function as SWR-053 — no second gate beside it, B033); when the gate blocks, the response shall **name** the withheld units so absence is distinguishable from emptiness (SWR-128 family). | SWR-185 | The Mission Control **header** shall carry a static link to the organisation page (`/organisation.html`); the link lives in `index.html` and **not** in `app.js` — the 111 JS assertions stay untouched, and a dead link is impossible because the page ships in the same static folder. Origin: client order 2026-08-21 („ich möchte diese Seite aus Mission Control aufrufen“), `platform/T-0043`. | STK-019 | Manual smoke (link renders, target 200) plus the existing static-serving path; no JS assertion is touched — that absence is the point and is checked by the unchanged `js_tests` count | medium | **reviewed** |

| SWR-186 | The HMI shall show **activities per role instance**: tickets the role completed, tickets still open/in review for it, tickets it reviews (reviewer field), and its runs from the run registry (provider, status, artefacts, time, cost) — across one unit or all. v1 explicitly does **not** claim prose remarks inside ticket bodies as activities: they carry no structured author/timestamp, and inventing either would be the SWR-183 mistake with a different face. | SWR-187 | Every unit shall define its **workflows in one machine-readable source** (`docs/workflows.yaml`): per workflow an id, name, takt, `geplant_von` (PL or the role itself), the recurring ticket it serves, governance marks (`arch_review`, `cm_verankert`) — and **steps**, each with `rolle` (or `script` plus `werkzeug`), `aktion`, `input` and `output`, where outputs are **as a rule work products** and shall reference CM-plan paths where they exist; a step output that names no known work product is **reported**, not invented. The HMI shall render each workflow as its step chain (input → role/tool → output) and shall list, per role, the workflows it participates in. **Every recurring (takt) ticket shall be covered by a workflow — an uncovered takt ticket is a reported gap** (SWR-128 family: the ground set is the takt tickets, not the workflow file). Origin: client order 2026-08-21 (mail-digest example: load mails (tool) → summarize (MAIL-RED) → dashboard → deliver), `platform/T-0044`. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_workflows`: schema parse, step chain integrity — every step has input and output, takt-ticket coverage pair: covered passes AND uncovered is reported, role filter, WP-reference check reports unknown outputs) plus server smoke against the seeded real workflows | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-188 | `projekt_setup.py` shall scaffold `docs/workflows.yaml` for every new project with the two standard takt workflows (PL monitoring, QM sample) **bound to the takt tickets it generates in the same run** — a new project starts with zero uncovered takt tickets. Origin: client order 2026-08-21 („für alle wiederkehrenden Tasks geplant und dargestellt“), `platform/T-0044`. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_projekt_setup`: workflows.yaml exists, references the two generated takt ticket ids, coverage check reports zero gaps on a fresh scaffold) | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-189 | The key of every entry in `process/roles/besetzungen.yaml` shall equal `<rolle>@<einheit>` built from that entry's own fields; a key contradicting its fields is a **finding**, and the check shall assert a non-empty ground set (SWR-128 family). The assertion shall read the **raw text**, not a normalising parser — the key is the object under test, and a parser that normalises it answers the question instead of asking it. This requirement stands **beside**, not instead of, the literal expectation in `test_am_echten_bestand_stehen_die_beiden_schnelltakt_besetzungen`: the literal catches a **rename**, this catches a **contradiction**, and a check that derives both from one source catches neither. Origin: Sprint 28 finding — the projektmodell rework renamed `PROB@aspice` → `PROB@platform` (HEAD carried key `@aspice` with field `einheit: platform`) and did not run the test that reads the real holding; the rework report claimed „78-Tests-Batch grün" — a **selection** that missed it. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_modellaufloesung.test_instanzschluessel_ist_rolle_at_einheit`: real holding has zero contradictions, ground set ≥ 1; counter-proof against a synthetic file carrying exactly the Sprint-27 HEAD contradiction) | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-190 | Goldset coverage shall be a **standing check**, not a plan: for every role with **at least one recorded run** in **any** run registry the organisation's discovery finds, the goldset shall hold at least `MINDESTFAELLE_JE_ROLLE` (20) documented cases, and a role below that is a **finding**. A role that appears in the goldset with **no** recorded run shall be **named as information, never as a finding** — turning it red would force exactly what `promt-team/T-0008` DoD 2 forbids: derived cases for roles there is nothing to hold them against. A **failed** run counts as a run: the question is whether the role is exercised in operation, not whether it went well (the Sprint-26 conflation, named here on purpose). The ground set shall be asserted non-empty (SWR-128 family), and the threshold shall live in **one** place read by the check rather than restated in it (SWR-131). Origin: `promt-team/T-0008`, fourth touch — the ticket waited three sprints for a fresh run while its DoD asked about roles that **have** runs, and those were covered on the day it was written. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_goldset.AbdeckungTest`: real holding has zero under-covered roles; ground set non-empty for both runs and cases; roles without runs are neither findings nor under-covered; a failed-run role still counts as having runs; counter-proof on a synthetic root where a role with a run and zero cases **is** reported) | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-191 | Every gate that asks *„is this working copy committed?"* shall compare the **tree** against `HEAD`, never the **index**: the check shall run `git status --porcelain -uall -b` against a temporary index seeded by `git read-tree HEAD` and located **outside** the repository, falling back to the real index only when seeding fails (repo without a commit), and that fallback shall be named at the one place it happens. A locked `.git/index.lock` that cannot be unlinked (R7, `SWR-163/164`) freezes the real index at the state **before** the last commit, and the gate then reports `MM` for files byte-identical to `HEAD`. The stale index shall be reported as its **own message with its own wording** and shall **not** raise the blocking count — the caller cannot remove the lock from the sandbox, and a permanent unfixable finding is exactly the construction that cost `SWR-166` 83 aborted pushes and 12 ticks that never ran. One implementation, shared by every caller (B033). Origin: `platform/T-0046` — the Sprint-28 preflight reported 2 findings (`platform` 3 files, `pm` 1 file) over work that was fully committed. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_preflight_baum.BaumStattIndexTest`: on a synthetic repo with a deliberately stale index the tree comparison reports clean while the real index reports dirty; the temp index is created outside the repo and removed again; seeding failure falls back instead of raising; `tick.arbeitskopie_sauber` calls `preflight.repo_status` and holds no second copy of the logic; the stale-index notice does not raise the blocking count) | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-192 | Comments on a task shall live in the **task file itself**, under one heading (`board.KOMMENTAR_UEBERSCHRIFT`, defined once and read by writer, reader and assertion), newest first, dated from the single time source (`board.zeitpunkt`, SWR-084) — never in a second file, table or store (B033, paid three times: `p9/T-0007`, `pm/T-0017`, `platform/T-0028`). A comment is **not an edit**: it shall change **no** frontmatter field including `geändert`, shall write **no** `Bearbeitet` note, shall **not** regenerate `BOARD.md`, and shall be accepted on `done`/`rejected` tasks — the `SWR-077` archive lock governs the **form, not the conversation**, and a closed task is the most common occasion for a question. It shall use the **same** write path as the editor (`git_schreiben.verbuche`, commit immediately, roll back the file if the commit fails, SWR-078/134), the **same** fingerprint conflict check as the editor (SWR-080, refreshed after each comment or the guard is worthless from the second comment on), and shall sit behind the single PIN gate at the head of `do_POST` rather than a second gate of its own. The client sends the fingerprint because it sends **text** — the inverse of the `SWR-144` argument that lets the terminate button read its own. Ordering is decided in **one** place (the backend); the view shall not re-sort. Origin: `platform/T-0030`, fourth touch — brief `platform/N-0007`: *„direkte kommentare/antworten … ähnlich wie hier beim Team-Chat"*, and the Team-Chat's defining property is that it is **not a second store**. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_ticket_kommentar`: 22 checks — no frontmatter field moves, no `geändert` jump, no `Bearbeitet` note, no `BOARD.md`; newest-first; reader and writer agree; accepted on `done` **and** `rejected` while the archive lock still rejects an ordinary edit; wrong fingerprint → 409, fingerprint moves after each comment; empty comment rejected; ticket still validates; full path incl. commit on a real git repo leaves the working copy clean and the status `done`) · JS tests (`ticket_verlauf.test.cjs`: 6 checks — the box is present on `done` **and** `rejected`, order comes from the backend and is **not** re-sorted in the view, comment text runs through the same renderer as the body) | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-193 | `blocked_by` shall accept a **qualified** reference `<einheit>/T-xxxx` — the same form `aggregation.ref` produces (SWR-087) and `SWR-176` already prefers over the bare id — resolved through the organisation's discovery, so that a block by a ticket in another repository is **expressible**. The bare `T-xxxx` shall remain repo-local and unchanged: a form is **added**, none replaced. Resolution shall distinguish **three** situations and treat only one as a finding: unit present and ticket present → silent; unit present and ticket **absent** → **finding**; organisation root or unit **not findable** → **no finding**, because that is a statement about the environment and not about the ticket (`board.py --check` runs in singly checked-out repos and in CI). The root shall be the **topmost** level that both carries ≥ 2 units **and lists the repo itself among them** — a count alone says „several folders are here", not „and one of them is me". A reference that does not match the qualified form shall never be turned into a path. Origin: `platform/T-0045` — Sprint 28 tried to book `promt-team/T-0003` as `blocked` and the tool refused **twice**; the same ticket had carried „no `blocked` — nothing is missing" through **four** terminations, which read like an assessment and was the only status the tool allowed. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_sperre_ueber_repos`: 12 checks — qualified block accepted alone and beside a local one; unit-present-ticket-absent is a finding naming both halves; unknown unit and unfindable root are **not** findings; bare id stays repo-local; `blocked` without reference and self-reference still rejected; the pattern matches the SWR-087 form and rejects `..`, absolute and malformed ids so none becomes a path; root search reaches the org root from inside the collection repo `projects/`) | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-194 | A lesson that states a **countermeasure** shall have a **representative**: an assertion whose source cites its lesson id. The ground set shall be the **honest subset** — lessons carrying an explicit `**Regel:**` line, a convention the corpus already uses (measured: **34 of 108**) — never all lessons, because most are observations and demanding a representative for each produces permanent findings, and a permanent finding trains the looking-away `SWR-166` was built against. The check shall be a **ratchet, not an indictment**: the measured backlog is held as a **named set** (not a count — a count cannot tell a swap from stasis, `L-2026-08-20by`) and a **new** unrepresented lesson is a finding that **names it**; a lesson that gains a representative is likewise a finding, so the progress is **booked** rather than silently absorbed. Ticket files shall **not** count as representatives — a ticket is an intention with a date, an assertion is its execution, and counting the former would answer the finding with the thing that caused it. The check shall be excluded from its **own** corpus. Origin: `platform/T-0034` — `L-003` (2026-08-06) was exemplary, filed **three** times, carried an expected value („repeat rate in Sprint 2 = 0"), and had **zero** effect for **14 days**: measured repeat rate **3 of 3**. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_lehren_vertreter`: 8 checks — ground set non-empty **and** strictly smaller than all lessons; no new unrepresented lesson, named if any; a gained representative must be booked; the checker and its own test are excluded from the corpus, with a counter-proof that the exclusion **works** — the test file lists all 29 ids in clear text, so without the exclusion the set would be empty and the check tautologically green; parking lot, `.git` and `node_modules` excluded; `.md` is not a representative extension) | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-195 | No decision log shall carry the same id twice. The check shall read **every** log the organisation's discovery finds (SWR-128 family, not a list someone typed into a ticket) and shall match **both** `D` and `B` rows — they share one table, and reading only `D` checks half the file. The measured legacy (`pm`: `D005` ×3, `D006` ×2) shall be held as a **named** exception and shall **not** raise a finding: the log is append-only and history is not rewritten here (Playbook Kap. 16), which places it in the same category as the carried-forward status transitions — reported, by name, non-blocking (SWR-166). A **new** duplicate shall be a finding naming **unit and id**; the named legacy **disappearing** shall likewise be a finding, because append-only means a line does not quietly vanish. Origin: `platform/T-0036` — `_naechste_d_id` forms `max + 1` and **cannot** produce a duplicate, so those five rows were hand-written: there is a **second write path** into the decision log and it has no number assignment (B033 with a *write path* as the forgotten copy). | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_entscheidungslog_dubletten`: 5 checks — ground set counts the **logs found**, not the duplicates, so a clean holding is distinguishable from broken discovery; no new duplicate, named if any; the named legacy must still be present; the row pattern reads `D` and `B` and rejects other prefixes and non-table lines; counter-proof on a synthetic root where an artificial duplicate **is** reported and a clean log is not) | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-196 | The tick's ticket selection shall apply the **occupancy** criterion as a **candidate filter**, not as a veto after the fact. When a motor is forced (`--provider`), `waehle_ticket` shall admit only tickets whose role has an occupancy with that motor in that unit, evaluating **every** candidate rather than the top-ranked one alone. The post-selection check (`besetzungsbefund`, SWR-171) shall remain in place unchanged as the second line for the **forced** selection (`--ticket`), which the filter never sees; both shall call the **same** resolver, so the expression exists once (not B033: one question asked of two different sets, not two copies of one answer). When no candidate qualifies, the message shall state the **stock**, not the exemplar: how many open tickets were examined, which roles they carry, and which instances hold that motor in that unit (SWR-167 — the result word carries the reason). Origin: `platform/T-0048` — measured 2026-08-21 at 04:15, the first run after `SWR-191`: preflight `STARTKLAR`, and **2 of 2** ticks still ended without result because the top-ranked candidate (`CM@platform`, `DEV@team-mail`) had no ollama occupancy; **0 of 8** open tickets organisation-wide carry an ollama-occupied role. A true-but-narrow message about a structural condition renews the hope of a different answer on every run — the twin of `L-2026-08-21ce`. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_tick_besetzungsfilter`: the filter finds a **rear** candidate behind an unoccupied top-ranked one — the counter-proof that it filters instead of vetoing; no qualifying role ⇒ no candidate; without `--provider` the selection is unchanged; `besetzungsbefund` still fires on forced `--ticket`; the message names count, roles and occupied instances; counter-proof on a **synthetic** root, L-2026-08-20cm) | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-197 | The set of decision ids assigned in **more than one unit** shall not grow. The check shall derive the assignment from **every** decision log the discovery finds (SWR-128 family) and compare it against the **named** legacy measured 2026-08-21: `D000`–`D013`, fourteen ids, each held by several units. A **new** cross-unit id shall be a finding naming the id and the units; a named legacy id **disappearing** shall likewise be a finding (append-only, as in SWR-195). The legacy itself shall **not** raise a finding — 214 citation sites cannot be repaired and a standing finding trains the looking-away SWR-166 was built against. The check shall additionally **report** the citation figures without blocking (SWR-174: the number carries its timestamp). Origin: `platform/T-0047`, preliminary question 1 executed: of **1023** unprefixed `D` citations, **743 (73 %)** sit inside the owning repo and are not ambiguous, **65 (6 %)** sit outside but name an id assigned only once, **214 (21 %)** are genuinely ambiguous — and **all 214 name one of those fourteen ids**. From `D014` upward every id is globally unique, so an unprefixed citation is already unambiguous there; the defect is a **prefix of the number range**, not a property of the corpus. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_entscheidungs_ids`: the ground set counts the **logs found**, not the duplicates; the named legacy is present and silent; a new cross-unit id is reported with id and units; a vanished legacy id is reported; the citation report separates inside/resolvable/ambiguous and its three parts sum to the total; counter-proof on a synthetic root) | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-198 | A ticket the team **cannot move** shall be exempt from **both** date checks, and the exemption shall be attached to its **state**, not to a stand-in for it. `sprint.sprint_vergangen` (SWR-112) and `aggregation._ist_unterminiert` (SWR-114/125) shall both skip a ticket that is `status: blocked` **and** carries a `blocked_by` reference (SWR-193) — bound to the **reference**, never to the bare word, because "blocked" without a reference is a claim and an exemption listening to a word alone is a loophole. The justification shall exist **once**, in `board.gesperrt`, and both checks shall **call** it rather than restate it (B033: two justifications for one thing drift apart); repairing one check alone merely **relocates** the finding. Origin: `platform/T-0051` — measured at the close of sprint 30, a blocked ticket had **no admissible value** for `geplant_sprint`: a past sprint raises "open on a past sprint", an empty one raises "ticket without sprint", and the only value silencing both is a delivery promise over someone else's action. `sprint.py` already exempted **one type**, `decision-request`, with literally this justification — it sat on a *type* standing in for a *state*, because until SWR-193 (one sprint old) `decision-request` was the only way to express "the team cannot act here". **A stand-in that long coincided with the thing becomes a hole the moment the thing gets its own name** (same family as SWR-196). Measured after the build at the real stock: preflight findings **1 → 0**, `unterminierte_tickets` 3 → 0, `sprint_vergangen` unchanged 0. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_gesperrt_terminzange`, 15 checks): **paired** per check — blocked ⇒ silent AND unblocked ⇒ still reported, since a check measuring only absence is green after a clear-cut too (SWR-148 form); `blocked` without `blocked_by` is reported by **both** checks (the loophole, decided instead of assumed) and `validiere` is asserted to reject it, so the reference binding costs nothing today and goes red if the validator is softened; the pincer case (old **and** empty date silent for the same blocked stock); the flat payload row carries `blocked_by` after `_ticket` is dropped; **build-form assertions** that both modules call `board.gesperrt` and that the key sentence of the justification appears in **exactly one** operational file. Counter-proof executed: with `board.gesperrt` forced to `False` (the pre-SWR-198 state) **5** assertions go red. Synthetic root (L-2026-08-20cm). | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-199 | The ground set of the lesson-representative check (SWR-194) shall be **every** lesson except those **explicitly** marked `**Beobachtung:**`, replacing the `**Regel:**` spelling filter, and the measured stock shall be held as a **named** legacy that stays silent while any **growth** is a finding naming the lesson. The opt-out shall be an **act**, not a side effect of punctuation. Measured 2026-08-21 over `process/knowledge/*/lessons.md`: **112** lessons, **38** carrying `**Regel:**`, **111** carrying a rule in *some* spelling, `ohne_vertreter` **29** under the old ground set and **91** under both "any rule spelling" and "no filter at all" — the two candidate repairs are **indistinguishable in effect**, so the filter is dropped rather than widened (of two equivalent forms, the one with fewer concepts). Representative rates confirm from the other side: **24 %** inside the 38 recognised, **15 %** among the 73 overlooked — the subset that *needs* a representative looks the same on both sides, so the convention separated punctuation, not substance. Origin: `platform/T-0050` — three lessons of sprint 30 (`cj`, `ck`, `cl`) were invisible because they read `**Regel.**`, **and the check stayed green**: a ratchet one evades by placing a colon differently is none. The assertion guarding that the convention *separates* read `len(mit_regel) < len(alle)` and would have passed at **111 of 112** — an inequality written where a ratio was meant. | STK-019 | Unit tests (`test_lehren_vertreter`, 12 checks): the ratio assertion (>90 % of lessons carry some rule) replaces the inequality and goes red if the stock moves; widening and dropping yield the **same** finding set; the `**Beobachtung:**` marker matches only at line start and the counter-proof shows the same text without it stays in the ground set; **zero** lessons currently carry the marker, so silently parking a finding as an "observation" is itself reported; named legacy of **91** with both directions (growth ⇒ finding, shrinkage ⇒ book the progress); the existing tautology guards (the check and its own test excluded from the corpus, parking lot excluded, tickets are not representatives) unchanged. Counter-proof executed: with the ground set forced back to `mit_regel` **2** assertions go red. | high | **reviewed** |

| SWR-200 | The set of code paths that **write** a decision log shall be a **named** set (`inbox.py` — appends the row and draws the number via `_naechste_d_id`; `pool.py` — creates the empty table on project start), and the number assignment shall exist at **exactly one** place; a change to either is a finding naming the file. Detection shall read the **syntax tree** and follow the assignment, not a single line of text.
